#2172. Astonishing moment manager HEADBUTTS opposition player and is sent off, sparking mass brawl as FA Cup first round clash descends into chaos

By JAMES DUTTON, DEPUTY SPORTS NEWS EDITOR and WILL GOVE

Published: 16:39 EST, 8 November 2026 | Updated: 16:41 EST, 8 November 2026

Oldham boss Micky Mellon was sent off for headbutting Sheffield Wednesday midfielder Callum Slattery as their FA Cup first round clash descended into a mass brawl at Boundary Park.

League Two Oldham pulled off a shock over their League One opponents, but the result was overshadowed by the altercation which occurred six minutes from the end of normal time.

The former Blackpool and Burnley midfielder appeared to grab Slattery by the back of the neck and pulled him closer to his face, before pushing his head into the rival player’s face and knocking him over.

The touchline soon descended into chaos with players and staff from both teams grappling with each other in a farcical melee.

Eventually the two warring sides were separated giving referee Martin Woods the opportunity to approach Mellon and present him with a thoroughly deserved red card.

Oldham, now with their manager in the stands, played on and incredibly nicked a 110th minute winner through Will Sutton.

Oldham Athletic manager Micky Mellon is shown a red card for moving his head towards Wednesday's Callum Slattery 🟥📺 Stream on TNT Sports & HBO Max: https://t.co/fRCRh6tXYB pic.twitter.com/5QMxgLt0dO

Oldham manager Micky Mellon was sent off for an astonishing headbutt on Sheffield Wednesday midfielder Callum Slattery

The scene soon descended into chaos as both players and staff from both sides entered the melee

Eventually Mellon was shown a red card and will serve a three match touchline ban

Wednesday piled on the pressure in the final minutes desperately trying to find an equaliser but to no avail as Oldham held on and advanced into the next round.

Mellon, however, will now have to serve a three-match touchline ban meaning he will miss his side's next appearance in this year's FA Cup.

Calum Kavanagh had put Oldham ahead in the 66th minute with a fierce drive after Mike Fondop headed on Noah Chilvers’ right-wing cross – his sixth goal of the season.

Wednesday equalised ten minutes later through former Oldham forward Davis Keillor-Dunn.

Elsewhere in the FA Cup on Sunday, seventh-tier Wimborne Town stunned League Two Crawley 2-0 to reach the second round for the first time in their history.

The Dorset part-timers of the Southern League Premier Division South took the lead through Yang Seung-woo’s stunning strike after nine minutes, with Cameron Munn deflecting in Jackson King’s shot 11 minutes from time to seal the upset.

Stockport survived a scare to beat Cray Wanderers of the Isthmian League Premier Division 3-2.

The League One high-flyers were facing a shock exit when they fell two goals behind in the first half, a situation not helped by a red card for goalkeeper Luca Ashby-Hammond for a foul outside the box.

From the free-kick, defender Alex Hernandez found the top corner to make it 2-0, but Stockport staged a late fightback, Kyle Wootton and, in added time, Mikael Mandron scoring to take the tie to extra time before Lewis Fiorini’s penalty won it.

Eighth-tier Stafford Rangers also led EFL opposition 2-0 before bowing out, Grimsby finding themselves facing an embarrassing exit against a team four divisions lower before hitting back to make it 3-3 after extra time, eventually going through on penalties.

#2166. HEARTS 2 FALKIRK 5: Wouter Vrancken slams 'childish' Hearts as five-star Bairns toy with injury-hit capital men at Tynecastle

Published: 16:57 EST, 8 November 2026 | Updated: 17:00 EST, 8 November 2026

Wouter Vrancken condemned a ‘ridiculous’ first-half display from his sorry Hearts side as their 18-month unbeaten home record in the Premiership was ripped apart by ruthless Falkirk.

The beleaguered Tynecastle side, who went into the game without 11 players through injury following a gruelling run of fixtures, endured a dismal afternoon as the Bairns raced into a 4-0 half-time lead on their way to a fifth Premiership win in a row.

Finley Barbrook, Owen Eames, Liam Henderson and Jacob Murrell’s double had the visitors 5-0 up within 47 minutes before Hearts reduced the deficit through substitute Sabri Guendouz and a Claudio Braga penalty.

It was a first league defeat at Tynecastle since April 2025 for the Gorgie men, who have now won just two of their last 11 matches in all competitions.

‘This is an embarrassment, the first half for sure,’ said Vrancken. ‘Always doubting, always doing things half-half, in possession, out of possession, everything, and such easy goals again.

‘When you give those easy goals, then you cannot win a game. It’s childish goals and cannot happen on this level. Those simple things, they are the basics actually in these situations.

American striker Jacob Murrell races off to celebrate his side's fifth goal on a day to remember

‘It’s crazy, crazy, and everybody — me, staff, players — have to take responsibility in those things. This is ridiculous.’

To exacerbate their grim predicament, Hearts lost yet another senior player as defender Malachi Fagan-Walcott limped off in the second half.

‘This also makes me a bit angry, because now we lose Mal again,’ said Vrancken. ‘Hopefully it’s not too bad, but still we had to replace him there, because he was one of the guys who was good in the game and in the last games actually he had a really consistent, good level.’

Hearts were leapfrogged into third place by Falkirk, who secured five consecutive top-flight victories for the first time in 31 years.

‘I’ve never been 5-0 up here before,’ said Bairns manager John McGlynn. ‘First half we were outstanding. A great start to the game for us to get the goal so early.

‘We got ourselves 4-0 up at half-time, which is dreamland. Hearts had a lot of the ball and dominated the second period, but we’re taking 5-2 every day of the week, make no mistake about it.’

Wouter Vrancken has been beset by injury concerns but he was in no mood for excuses

The Bairns seized the lead in the fourth minute. After Murrell’s close-range effort from Scott Tanser’s cross was brilliantly saved by Beau Reus, Barbrook reacted quickest to drill home a low, angled finish from eight yards out.

Falkirk doubled their lead in the 17th minute after the hosts were caught wide open on the counter-attack. Yan Dhanda’s shot from distance was charged down, allowing the Bairns to break swiftly, with Brad Spencer weighting his pass perfectly for Eames who fired past the exposed Reus.

Braga thought he had pulled one back for Hearts in the 20th minute, but it was ruled out for handball.

Falkirk took full advantage of this reprieve by adding a third in the 33rd minute as Murrell peeled off Fagan-Walcott at the back post to nod home Deniss Melniks’ cross from the right.

The visitors struck again in first-half stoppage-time as Henderson curled high past Reus after Tanser’s corner broke to him just inside the box.

Within two minutes of the restart Falkirk notched again as Murrell fired home after being set up by Ethan Ross.

Former Hearts boss John McGlynn led his side to a quite remarkable fifth consecutive win

Hearts got off the mark when Guendouz drilled in his first goal for the club from just inside the box in the 50th minute. Braga then tucked away a penalty in the 74th minute after Guendouz was tripped by Lewis Neilson, but it was far too little, far too late.

McGlynn added: ‘We had to smell blood and we had to go for it and that’s exactly what happened. Our play was outstanding in the first half. We won every duel, we won everything all over the park.

‘Winning five on the trot has not been done since 1995, Jim Jefferies’ team, so I’m delighted for everyone at the football club that we’ve managed to equal that record.’

Dalle ambizioni tricolori agli elogi per il centrocampista azzurro: le parole di Juan Sebastian Veron sul momento d'oro della Lazio.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

Perché è per caso vietato? Ditemi chi, in questo inizio di stagione, nel campionato di Serie A, ha mostrato di avere qualcosa in più della Lazio? Vi rispondo io: nessuno. Poi qualcosa potrà cambiare, magari subentrerà un po' di stanchezza nelle gambe dei ragazzi di Gattuso, ma le basi su cui è stato costruito il primato sono solide, molto solide.

In Italia ci sono pochi elementi con le sue capacità d'inserimento. E poi fa gol, che nel calcio non è mai un dettaglio.

#2160. Prickly Unai Emery fires back at 'more questions' from Sky Sports reporter in awkward interview after storming down Old Trafford tunnel before full time

By JAMES DUTTON, DEPUTY SPORTS NEWS EDITOR

Published: 16:35 EST, 8 November 2026 | Updated: 17:09 EST, 8 November 2026

Aston Villa boss Unai Emery was in a prickly mood as he bit back at Sky Sports reporter Patrick Davison in a fiery end to his post-match interview at Old Trafford on Sunday.

Emery had just seen his Villa side twice throw away a two-goal lead against Manchester United – with Joshua Zirkzee and Patrick Dorgu scoring in stoppage time to secure a remarkable 4-4 draw.

The Villa boss was spotted storming down the touchline before the full-time whistle and after Dorgu's dramatic 97th-minute equaliser – with play continuing for another two minutes.

And the Villa boss, already exasperated at being asked 'more questions,' did not take kindly to being asked if he was emotional about the late equaliser or if he felt it was unfair.

When asked if his walking off was an emotional reaction, he responded: 'No emotion. They scored in minute 96 and there were six minutes added. I [thought] the match was finishing.'

And then when asked whether the award of the goal was unfair, he replied: 'No, no. Not unfair. Please.

Unai Emery was in a prickly mood towards the end of his post match interview with Sky Sports

Emery had just stormed down the tunnel at Old Trafford after Aston Villa threw away a 4-2 lead in stoppage time

Emery took exception to being asked 'more questions' by Sky reporter Patrick Davison

'They scored the goal in minute 96. You were making the question [about why] I left before. Because the minute 96 and 30 seconds, was when we started the kick-off.'

Emery ended the interview making light of his 'poor' English, and then sarcastically asking if there were 'more questions' before an ironic 'Have a good Sunday' as he walked out of the interview.

Villa's director of football operations Damian Vidagany complained on social media about the legitimacy of United's last-gasp equaliser.

He accused United goalkeeper Senne Lammens – who went up for the corner – of impeding his Villa counterpart Zion Suzuki as he looked to punch the ball clear.

After Roy Keane said 'football is a contact sport,' he posted on X: 'To put in context this convenient narrative of "contact sport" we've been told by referees during the summer that this season to go to unbalance or push the GK on purpose on the six-yard box was not going to be allowed anymore.

'Lammens did not even look at the ball, went straight to do a contact with the hip over Zion.

'Having said that we played good match and we must do better than last six minutes. We will do and we will improve.

'But the rules are the rules and must be respected. That is a fact.'

Michael Carrick was involved in a spiky interview with Sky Sports about his Man United future

Sky Sports' Davison's brush with Emery came just minutes after a spiky interview with beleaguered United boss Michael Carrick.

When the future of his own position was put to him by Davison, Carrick took exception to the question.

'What do you expect to happen over the next few weeks? You've been asked time and again about your future? I know you have said that you walking away was the best for the club, you'd probably do it,' Davison asked Carrick.

'It's not going to happen, no,' Carrick replied.

With United chiefs Omar Berrada and Jason Wilcox in the stands, Davison pushed further asking: 'What do you expect to happen? Clearly there's going to be conversation?'

This prompted Carrick to quizzically state: 'Is there?' as the pair engaged in a back-and-forth on the subject.

Eventually Carrick replied: 'You know the situation, we haven't won enough games. But I expect to move forward with things we've been working on, showing a lot of the good from today tighten up a few mistakes and win some football matches.'

United's start of one win in 10 games is still their worst since Alex Ferguson replaced Ron Atkinson 40 years ago this week. They conceded twice at the start of each half against Villa, and have now let in 30 goals in all competitions – the joint highest in Europe's top five leagues.

#2157. Sebastiano Esposito, l’agente: “Ora non pensa al mercato, ma potrebbe giocare benissimo in un tridente con Hojlund e Vergara”

A Radio Napoli Centrale, nel corso di Un Calcio alla Radio, è intervenuto l’agente Mario Giuffredi: “Io vivo le partite dei miei assistiti con grande passione e coinvolgimento. Come se fossi con loro in campo. Durante la partita non riesco a essere distaccato, sono molto acceso. Dopo la partita, ritorna l’equilibrio. E riesco anche a essere più equilibrato delle critiche degli stessi calciatori. Come ho vissuto i due rigori degli Esposito? Non ho visto nessuno dei due rigori di Pio e Sebastiano. Ero anche a cena con Salvatore Esposito. Capivamo in base alle reazioni degli altri che erano con noi a tavola. Pio sentiva la responsabilità della partita. Aver segnato quel rigore contro la Francia toglieva il pensiero del rigore della Bosnia. Pio ha una lunga carriera, può migliorare statistiche con Nazionale e club. Io non guardo le pagelle, so come ragionano i giornalisti, che non si limitano a guardare la prestazione, ma anche episodi, dettagli. La prestazione di Sebastiano è stata saggia a livello tattico. Ha giocato in posizioni di campo non suoi. Mi dispiace che i giornalisti non si limitano a questo, ma si limitano a guardare il rigore sbagliato. Per sbagliare quel rigore, devi avere il coraggio di batterlo. Sull’1-1, contro quel portiere (Maignan ndr) e contro quella Nazionale, devi avere coraggio. Io mi ero arrabbiato con Sebastiano se non avesse avuto il coraggio di andare dal disco. Sebastiano Esposito e Vergara insieme con Hojlund? Si incastrerebbero benissimo, perché Sebastiano è un toro a livello fisico. Vergara ha grande forza nelle gambe. Hanno anche colpi tecnici, sono imprevedibili. Non sarebbe maschio. Però non facciamo i direttori del Napoli né gli allenatori del Napoli. Aiutare il Napoli portando Sebastiano Esposito? Io non mi sono mai sottratto ad aiutare il Napoli e mai lo farò. Al tempo stesso non sono io che vado a insistere per far prendere quel tipo di giocatore. Sono orgoglioso di quello che sono. Il fatto di essere arrivato dalla galetta mi rende orgoglioso. Negli ultimi due anni ho avuto la media di quattro giocatori convocati in Nazionale. Vedo che nel primo e nel secondo tempo ho due giocatori su tre in campo. Ieri sera dopo la partita, presa coscienza, ho preso l’auto e sono andato nei luoghi dove tutto è iniziato. Per un napoletano è tutto più difficile, ci sono tantissimi ostacoli messi davanti da invidiosi. Hai sentito Pio Esposito dopo la partita contro la Francia? Il futuro di Pio è all’Inter, senza dubbio. Sta benissimo. Il club ha mostrato sempre attacco e riconoscenza. Sebastiano si trova benissimo al Sassuolo, in questo momento non pensare neanche al mercato. Quello che succederà si vedrà. Entrambi stanno bene dove stanno. Pio sicuramente rimarrà all’Inter per tanti anni, è la sua seconda pelle; mentre Sebastiano magari si vedrà strada facendo se sia meritevole di alzare ancora di più il livello, ma è prematuro parlare di mercato.Il paragone di Pio Esposito con Meazza? Noi vediamo tutto con serenità ed equilibrio. Io vivo con questa concezione: ci addormentiamo la sera con la consapevolezza che il mattino possa finire tutto. Questo ci permette di non cullarci su certe cose. Il calcio è lo sport più bello e più crudo del mondo. Uno dei più grandi giocatori per me è stato Roberto Baggio. Lui a tratti viene dimenticato, nessuno gli ha dato un posto di un certo livello. Il calcio ha bisogno di gente come Roberto Baggio. Questo mi fa pensare che un giocatore verrà dimenticato in un secondo. Perciò bisogna vivere tutto con equilibrio. Qual è il prossimo che la mia scuderia ha in rampa di lancio? Te ne dico due. Quando dico le cose ci metto la faccia e vado anche controcorrente. Dico Favasuli e Marianucci. Per me Marianucci può arrivare in Nazionale, è un difensore di grandissimo livello. Ha qualcosa in più rispetto a tanti altri. Se migliora nell’attenzione, diventa di grande livello. Favasuli lo diamo per scontato, perché è partito bene”.

#2156. Gila sottolinea: “Si è calciatori h24, tutto deve portarti ad essere al 100% in partita”

Mario Gila, difensore del Milan, è il protagonista del secondo episodio di The Chamber, la serie di interviste di AC Milan dedicata al benessere mentale. Prima dell’intervista, Mario trascorre del tempo all’interno di una camera semi-anecoica, allontanandosi dal rumore e dalle distrazioni della vita quotidiana per concentrarsi sui propri pensieri. Subito dopo incontra Luca Casadei per una conversazione aperta sulla forza mentale, su ciò che si può imparare dalle esperienze difficili, sull’importanza di mantenere un atteggiamento positivo e continuare a credere in sé stessi. Di seguito un estratto delle sue parole.

Sei uno che ha bisogno di avere tutto sotto controllo?

"Credo che, per come vedo questa professione e per la posizione in cui ci troviamo noi calciatori, credo che – anche se non sembra – ci siano tante situazioni da gestire, come ti comporti fuori dal campo. In fondo sei un calciatore ventiquattro ore su ventiquattro: l'alimentazione, la preparazione atletica, gli allenamenti. Ed è come se dovessi tenere tutto perfettamente sotto controllo affinché nel weekend o nella partita infrasettimanale io sia al 100%. Quindi è un po' la professione stessa a portarti a tenere tutto sotto controllo, e se sei uno a cui piace fare così, finisci per accentuare ancora di più questa tendenza; e in queste situazioni avere tutto sotto controllo mi fa stare bene".

#2153. Xabi Alonso must look at HIMSELF, not the officials, as another questionable line-up sees Chelsea fold at Sunderland - and Cole Palmer wastes his chance to respond to Thomas Tuchel snub, writes KIERAN GILL

Published: 11:14 EST, 8 November 2026 | Updated: 17:34 EST, 8 November 2026

When searching for why Chelsea were beaten by the side who were sitting bottom of the Premier League, the temptation from Xabi Alonso might be to describe this defeat as a tale of two penalties.

First, Chelsea were denied one when Cole Palmer was sandwiched by Luke O'Nien and Jules Ahoka as he entered the Sunderland area, with the officials concluding the contact occurred outside of the box.

Then, Sunderland were awarded one when Brian Brobbey was brought down by Levi Colwill. The striker had hold of the defender at the same time, and arguably clipped his own heel upon second viewing, but Enzo Le Fee gladly accepted the opportunity to score the game's only goal.

However, it would be wrong to blame this deserved defeat on those two decisions alone. Referee Jarred Gillett and VAR Nick Hopton were weak but not the reason that Chelsea lost.

This was actually a tale of two strikers. Sunderland had one in the brilliant bully that is Brobbey, whereas Chelsea had nobody after Alonso yet again decided against starting Danny Welbeck.

In the three Premier League games played since Joao Pedro's injury – against Tottenham Hotspur, Manchester United and now Sunderland – Alonso has gone with Morgan Rogers as a false nine once and Pedro Neto twice. Welbeck was on the bench in all of those matches.

Sunderland handed Chelsea a brutal reality check as they climbed off the foot of the table

The visitors fell victim to two questionable penalty calls at the Stadium of Light

We are not saying the 35-year-old is the answer to all of Alonso's problems, but he is an actual No9 who scored 13 times in the Premier League for Brighton last season.

Neto was hardly going to have a high success rate when wrestling with centre backs for the long balls which were coming from Colwill and Co. It is a question that needs asking, therefore, especially after another loss.

Why did Chelsea sign Welbeck if not to play when Joao Pedro is injured?

Welbeck came on in the 62nd minute here, but by then, a familiar feeling was washing over Chelsea.

They lost 2-1 here at the very end of last season. Malo Gusto scored an own goal, Wesley Fofana was red carded, and Chelsea, managed by Calum McFarlane in the interim, failed to qualify for any form of European football.

They smacked of an utterly unserious side then, and resembled it here, too.

Chelsea had a week to prepare for this Premier League clash while Sunderland were playing – and losing – to Dinamo Zagreb in the Europa League.

Regis Le Bris' side had not won in the league since August. Yet they were the sharper of the two teams here.

It hardly helped Alonso that Chelsea's attackers never appeared at it individually.

Cole Palmer had a stinker. This was supposed to be an opportunity to show why Thomas Tuchel made a mistake in not naming him in his latest England squad. If anything, he helped make sure those accusations cannot be levelled at Tuchel over this break.

Xabi Alonso tinkered with his line-up and it failed to pay off in a second defeat in three games

Cole Palmer had a stinker in a woeful response to Thomas Tuchel's England snub

Rogers' performance was best summed up by the time he simply sprinted out of play with the ball to gift Sunderland a goal-kick. He and Palmer resembled no-shows.

Sunderland (4-2-3-1): Roefs 6; Hume 7, Mukiele 7, O'Nien 6, Mandava 7; Xhaka 6 (Ahoka 4, 6), Sadiki 7; Meunier 6, Talbi 6 (Rigg 88), Le Fee 6 (Angulo 88); Brobbey 8 (Isidor 67, 6)

Subs (not used): Ellborg, Danso, Methalie, Fofana, Tutierov

Booked: Ahoka, Meunier, Brobbey, O'Nien, Angulo

Chelsea (4-3-3): Penders 6; Palestra 5 (Gusto 62, 5), Fofana 5, Colwill 4 (Estevao 45, 6), Chavarria 5 (Quenda 79); Caicedo 6, Lavia 6, Henderson 6 (Emegha 87); Palmer 4, Neto 5 (Welbeck 62, 5), Rogers 4

Subs (not used): Sharman-Lowe, Barco, Anselmino, Nicoll-Jazuli

Neto looked like a lost little boy trying to lead Chelsea's line in the absence of Joao Pedro.

The half-time statistics said Chelsea had sent more open-play crosses into Sunderland's box in the opening 45 minutes than in their entire games v Fulham, Everton, Tottenham, Manchester United, Bournemouth and Brighton.

Except, they had no striker for Alonso's full backs Marco Palestra or Pep Chavarria to try to find. Just 5ft 8in Neto and the odd other box-crasher.

Trailing 1-0 at half-time here, Alonso made a singular substitution. It was to introduce Estevao for Colwill, who had been tormented by Brobbey.

Le Bris was asked in his post-match press conference if there is another player like his striker in the Premier League. He thought long and hard before answering: 'What do you think? I don't think so. His physicality, his intensity, it's impressive, that's for sure. The build-up is so important to have a target man.'

Alonso chipped in on Brobbey: 'He is not unstoppable. He is stoppable but only when you defend better than we did.' On his own attack, he added: 'We didn't produce enough to deserve to win.'

Sunderland's tactic of hoofing the ball up to Brobbey worked wonders. He scared the daylights out of Chelsea's centre backs. When Fofana first arrived at the Stadium of Light, a Chelsea fan shouted at him as he got off the bus. 'Fofana, please be on the bench,' he said. Fofana may have been wishing he was a substitute as well whenever he was wrestling with Brobbey.

It was the long-ball approach which led to Sunderland winning their penalty. They smashed it towards the top of the pitch and Brobbey bullied Colwill. Sunderland won their spot-kick and, in truth, deserved the lead for the sheer energy they brought to this match.

Alonso is ten Premier League games into his Chelsea career. The honeymoon period is over. Sunderland lost their captain Granit Xhaka to an injury after two minutes here. Chelsea had named a line-up featuring three midfielders. But they never showed any signs of control, and for that, Alonso should be looking at himself and his own side rather than the refereeing team.

#2149. Napoli, occhi sempre su Zirkzee: duello con la Juve per Varela. Il punto di Mediaset

Negli ultimi giorni sono molti i nomi che sono finiti sul taccuino del ds Giovanni Manna. L’ultimo, quello di Joshua Zirkzee. Il Napoli continua a lavorare sul mercato alla ricerca di un rinforzo offensivo da affiancare a Hojlund. Il più seguito è Varela, ma arrivare al centravanti del Monza già a gennaio non sarà semplice. Le sue nove reti, che ne fanno il capocannoniere della Serie A, saranno fondamentali nella corsa salvezza della squadra di Juric. Privarsi del proprio attaccante più prolifico a metà stagione rappresenterebbe un sacrificio importante per il club brianzolo. Una difficoltà che spinge Manna a guardarsi intorno e a valutare anche soluzioni alternative.

Su Varela, intanto, si registra anche l’interesse della Juventus, dando vita a un possibile derby di mercato tra bianconeri e azzurri. Il Napoli segue e apprezza il 21enne portoghese: nelle scorse ore il club ha raccolto informazioni attraverso il suo entourage, approfondendo anche gli aspetti economici dell’operazione. La concorrenza e soprattutto il peso del giocatore negli equilibri del Monza rendono però la trattativa particolarmente complicata.

Ecco perché Manna sta battendo anche altre piste, con uno sguardo rivolto soprattutto alla Premier League, dove non mancano occasioni da cogliere. Una porta a Joshua Zirkzee, 25 anni, attualmente al Manchester United. L’olandese conosce bene la Serie A, dove si era messo in mostra nella stagione 2023/24 con la maglia del Bologna. Sotto la guida di Thiago Motta aveva realizzato 11 gol e 5 assist, contribuendo alla storica qualificazione dei rossoblù in Champions League. Numeri e prestazioni che avevano convinto lo United a investire 42,5 milioni di euro per portarlo a Old Trafford.

A Manchester, però, il percorso dell’attaccante ha subito una battuta d’arresto. In questa stagione ha trovato poco spazio, collezionando appena 186 minuti e due reti in dieci partite. Una situazione che potrebbe favorire un’eventuale partenza e trasformare l’olandese in un’opportunità nel mercato di gennaio. Il Napoli osserva e vuole provarci: l’olandese potrebbe rappresentare una soluzione per completare il reparto e offrire a Hojlund un compagno e ad Allegri un’alternativa con caratteristiche diverse rispetto all’attaccante danese.

Per arrivare a Zirkzee, però, il Napoli dovrà prima fare i conti anche con il mercato in uscita. Le posizioni di Lang e Lucca sono quelle da monitorare con maggiore attenzione: entrambi potrebbero lasciare spazio a nuovi innesti e diventare le prime pedine sacrificabili per fare cassa. Eventuali cessioni permetterebbero a Manna di recuperare risorse da reinvestire sul mercato e provare a dare concretezza all’ipotesi che porta all’attaccante dello United. La strada per Zirkzee passa dunque anche dalle uscite: il Napoli valuta le occasioni in entrata, ma prima dovrà trovare le condizioni economiche per affondare il colpo.

#2147. Aberdeen boss Stephen Robinson is doomed... a haunted man whose players have given up the ghost

Published: 16:16 EST, 8 November 2026 | Updated: 17:42 EST, 8 November 2026

Stephen Robinson has looked like a beaten man for weeks. The Aberdeen manager insists he is still up for the fight but the body language suggests otherwise: tired, haunted and so out of explanations for his team’s wretched form that he doesn’t even try to come up with any more.

It is inconceivable that he will continue in the post after another traumatic defeat at Pittodrie yesterday. Not only did Aberdeen lose to Hibs, they gave up the ghost when a second goal went in with 11 minutes left and eventually succumbed to a 4-0 drubbing by the team who had started the day bottom of the league.

Whatever he might say to the contrary, Robinson would probably be just as minded to chuck it, were it not for the compensation that will come his way when he is sacked in the days ahead. And sacked he surely will be, especially now that the international window gives the club some breathing space.

Watching the usually chirpy Robinson have the stuffing knocked out of him by this bruising experience has not been fun. With every passing week, he has looked increasingly worn down by the weight of the club, the wrath of supporters (who never wanted him there in the first place) and the half-hearted efforts of his own players.

Quite apart from their three league wins from 12 and the home defeats by St Johnstone and Falkirk, there has been no sign of a playing style that showed potential. Nobody expected sophisticated football from the Northern Irishman, but even the aggression, physicality and direct approach for which he was renowned elsewhere has been conspicuous by its absence.

Robinson’s Aberdeen seem to be better out of possession, as they showed against Rangers in the Premier Sports Cup semi-final, than they are in it. After conceding an early goal to St Johnstone last month, they were given the ball for the next 76 minutes but had no idea what to do with it.

Aberdeen boss Stephen Robinson shows his despair during the 4-0 defeat by HIbs at Pittodrie

It seems almost inconceivable that Robinson will stay in his post after another traumatic loss

Hibs striker Thibault Klidje rounds off the scoring as Aberdeen collapse in the closing stages

You might get away with that at St Mirren and Motherwell, where he had so much success in previous years, but it’s no use at a club of Aberdeen’s stature. By appointing a manager who was the polar opposite of his predecessor, Jimmy Thelin, the board at Pittodrie had hoped to go back to basics, but now even the fundamentals have gone.

Owner Dave Cormack has tried everything since becoming Aberdeen’s chairman in 2019. Stephen Glass and Barry Robson were young project managers. Thelin was the foreigner with a philosophy. Jim Goodwin and Robinson were the guys with a track record in the Premiership.

All failed, even Thelin, whose historic Scottish Cup triumph had promised so much. At times like these, Alex Ferguson’s miraculous achievements of the 1980s are blamed for casting a long shadow over Pittodrie, but a successful past is no excuse for a failed future.

After all, Derek McInnes was not so weighed down by history that he couldn’t guide Aberdeen to four second-place finishes. Before that, Jimmy Calderwood led them into the last 32 of the UEFA Cup. Goodwin is now faring rather well with Dundee United. And whatever is said about Robinson, he deserved a shot at one of the big jobs.

At what point is it acknowledged that the problem is not so much the managers as the mistakes made by those who appoint them? Aberdeen are lucky to have a benefactor like Cormack who has invested millions in the team and its infrastructure, but the money hasn’t bought them a vision that could be turned into reality.

In 2024, Aberdeen appointed a German consultancy firm to conduct a holistic, top-to-bottom review of their football operations. They appointed Thelin and drew up a three-year plan that would modernise the club and lead it to a sustainable long-term future. One of those who assisted in that process, Lutz Pfannenstiel, later became the club’s sporting director.

Whatever happened to all that? When Thelin ran out of road, Aberdeen’s chief executive, Alan Burrows, turned to Robinson, with whom he had worked at Motherwell. It never felt like he would fit into a framework that had been built around his predecessor, a Swede whose management style could hardly have been more different.

Among the many complaints from disgruntled Aberdeen fans are questions about recruitment. More particularly, who is signing which players? Let’s just say they have their doubts that Pfannenstiel scoured the whole of Europe last summer and came up with Brad Lyons and Toyosi Olusanya.

This is a club that has lost its way, an institution that appears to have given up on joining the dots between its various departments. The crumb of comfort for disillusioned fans is that a new chief executive is just in the door and will surely see this as an opportunity to address the malaise. The role vacated by Burrows in September is now filled by former FA communications executive Adrian Bevington.

Assuming Robinson is asked to clear his desk this week, the challenge for Bevington will not just be to replace him. It won’t just be a matter of getting together with his decision-making colleagues and weighing up a data-based appointment from the Netherlands. Or using his negotiating skills to lure John McGlynn from Falkirk.

It will be about the club as a whole. About ensuring that everyone at Aberdeen is singing from the same hymn sheet, with shared goals, a common vision and a manager who fits into the grand plan. It won’t guarantee them success, but it will bring about progress. And right now, that is as much as Aberdeen can ask.

Invitato al programma "Menu Latour", Medhi Benatia ha parlato a lungo del suo periodo come dirigente dell'Olympique Marsiglia. E tra promesse non mantenute e questioni irrisolte, l'opinionista di CANAL+ non ha usato mezzi termini nel criticare Pablo Longoria. L'ex direttore sportivo dell'OM ha spiegato che tutto è cambiato lo scorso gennaio, durante la trasferta in Kuwait per la Supercoppa di Francia contro il PSG, quando, in un incontro con l'ex presidente del Marsiglia Pablo Longoria e il proprietario del club Frank McCourt, ha appreso della situazione finanziaria della società e della necessità di vendere giocatori per 30 milioni di euro: "È stato allora che ho capito che dovevamo vendere. In Kuwait, mi sono reso conto che le cose non andavano bene".

"Non avevo ancora deciso di andarmene. Ero molto infastidito. Mi sono sentito tradito. Quando i conti non coincidono, mi piace discuterne. Nessuno può mentire o eludere la questione. La differenza, per me, è che quando ho lasciato Marsiglia non ho chiesto nulla, non ho firmato alcun accordo di riservatezza. Non c'era bisogno di comprare il mio silenzio. Quello che ho da dire è troppo costoso; la verità non ha prezzo. Capisco la frustrazione della gente. Ci hanno dato tanto affetto. Sono stati pazienti. Hanno creduto in questo progetto. È questo che fa male ai marsigliesi oggi".

Grazie alla sua rete di contatti, Benatia cercò comunque di portare diversi giocatori di alto livello riducendo i costi, citando alcuni esempi: "Pavard? Gli pagavamo il 35% del suo stipendio all'Inter. Tre mesi prima giocava una finale di Champions League. Højbjerg? Guadagna 3,5 milioni di euro all'anno al Tottenham, quanto pensate di dargli come stipendio? Se solo sapeste quanti messaggi di congratulazioni ho ricevuto alla fine del mercato. Il calcio è incredibile. Ci sono persone che non sbagliano mai".

Published: 16:07 EST, 8 November 2026 | Updated: 18:07 EST, 8 November 2026

Crystal Palace manager Pierre Sage is facing the sack after his side dropped to the bottom of the Premier League with Thomas Frank in pole position to replace him.

Palace have won just two of their ten league matches this season, with Sunday's 3-1 defeat to Liverpool leaving them at the foot of the league for the first time in nearly nine years.

Sage is expected to become the first Premier League manager sacked this season and former Spurs boss Frank is considered the frontrunner to take over.

Sean Dyche, though, also has his admirers at Selhurst Park.

However, Sage believes his side have been unlucky with results this season and has requested the hierarchy remain patient.

The 47-year-old, who left RC Lens for Palace in the summer to succeed Oliver Glasner, also admitted the Palace squad needs strengthening - perhaps to absolve himself of some blame.

Crystal Palace are set to sack Pierre Sage with the club bottom of the Premier League

'You can create the links between these two things,' Sage replied when asked whether being bottom of the league heading into the international break could see him sacked.

'But a lot of times I've said we deserved better and it was the case again on Sunday.

'My job is not the most important thing, as I always said from the beginning. The most important thing is to find an efficient solution for the team and for the club.

'I know how important it is for the club to stay in the Premier League. The club just have to believe in that, and if it's not the case, they will take a decision.

'I always have confidence in myself because it's a big, big mistake to not be 100% involved in what I have to do.

'We have to adjust some positions during the winter transfer window to give more solutions and more efficiency and I think it will be okay.'

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

Maradona junior ha descritto così l’inizio della partita: «Credo che i primi 15 minuti del Napoli contro la Lazio siano stati terrificanti. La Lazio ci ha messo seriamente alle corde. Poi pian piano il fatto di non prendere gol ci ha dato fiducia e abbiamo iniziato a giochicchiare».

Nel suo giudizio, dunque, la Lazio ha messo in difficoltà il Napoli fin dalle prime battute. La squadra azzurra ha evitato di subire gol in quella fase e, secondo Maradona junior, ha trovato gradualmente più fiducia.

L’ex calciatore ha distinto l’avvio, definito terrificante, dai minuti successivi. Pur riconoscendo che il Napoli abbia cominciato a giocare dopo aver resistito alla pressione iniziale, ha scelto il verbo giochicchiare per descrivere quella fase della prestazione.

Inter ecco il 'nuovo' Bastoni: più leader e più sicuro, così sta crescendo come centrale puro

Installa la nostra App gratuita e accedi più velocemente alle notizie

Mentre navighi nell'app, scorri il dito da sinistra a destra dello schermo per tornare alle pagine precedenti

Alcune prove opache, poi il salto di qualità. Alessandro Bastoni ha iniziato a sfoderare grandi prestazioni anche da centrale puro. Il difensore quest'anno ha giocato tre volte in quella posizione e ha sempre convinto. Ha limato qualche difetto, alzato la soglia dell'attenzione e si è dimostrata pedina duttile per Cristian Chivu e Roberto Mancini.

"Henry Ford e Alessandro Bastoni non sono mai stati così d’accordo. Il primo, fondatore della Ford, sosteneva che “chiunque impari qualcosa resta giovane”. Ale annuisce sereno: i miglioramenti si vedono. Quest’anno, al netto di qualche partita un po’ così, sta limando quei difetti che ne accompagnano il cammino da anni. Uno su tutti, riassunto in una frase: “Come braccetto è bravo, ma se c’è da fare il centrale dei tre…”. Una tesi alimentata dopo l’espulsione di Zenica contro la Bosnia, in una partita giocata da perno di una linea a tre completata da Mancini e Calafiori", sottolinea la Gazzetta dello Sport.

Una crescita silenziosa, importante, di spessore, alimentata anche da una sana rivincita sportiva. L’espulsione contro la Bosnia è una ferita aperta, così come l’eliminazione ai playoff e il mancato accesso al Mondiale per la terza volta di fila, ma Bastoni non ne ha mai parlato in toni apocalittici. La miglior vittoria è una sola: pensare sempre a fare meglio, limando difetti e costruendo un “nuovo Bastoni”, bravo anche come centrale difensivo. Nell’Inter, affiancato da Akanji e Carlos – a Venezia ha giocato Bisseck al posto dello svizzero – ha guidato la difesa senza sbavature in due occasioni su tre (male solo contro lo Shakhtar). Sta migliorando in leadership e sicurezza. Una buona notizia per Chivu e anche per Mancini".

Leeds have promised to push for legal action to be taken against the abusers if they can be identified

#2131. Man United are a rotten, shambolic mess. They lack intelligence, look uncoached and fumbling, failing Michael Carrick is not the man to arrest this trauma, writes IAN LADYMAN

Published: 14:32 EST, 8 November 2026 | Updated: 20:25 EST, 8 November 2026

Manchester United do not have a football team, more a collection of adrenaline junkies. Over time, it will not be enough.

So deficient are Michael Carrick's players that they surely can't keep their manager in a job for much longer. For the most part United are shambolic, a mess. At times they play football so devoid of intelligence and competence that it's almost impossible to fathom. They look uncoached.

Yet here they were for the second night running at Old Trafford, completing a lap of honour at full-time while their supporters sang the name of Carrick as he completed his own journey in front of the Stretford End towards the tunnel.

United – it's worth pointing out – are third bottom of the Premier League. They haven't won in the league since August and have conceded more goals across all competitions – 30 – than any team bar Crystal Palace in Europe's top divisions.

But at Old Trafford they will cling to anything to help them look in the mirror without laughing and currently the currency is comebacks. There was one against Roma in the Champions League last Tuesday and here – quite remarkably – there were two in one game.

None of it should really mean anything in the grand scheme. United are rotten, capable of playing effectively only when something gives them an energy spike with which to roll. It makes for quite hypnotic theatre but has inadequacy and desperation at its core.

Patrick Dorgu (centre) scored a late equaliser as troubled Man United drew 4-4 vs Aston Villa

Dorgu scored in the 96th-minute with this header but United sit 18th in the Premier League

Despite the draw, Michael Carrick's position as United head coach looks increasingly doubtful

It won't sustain them or indeed their fumbling, failing manager. But for the moment it's what they have and what they are.

MANCHESTER UNITED (4-2-3-1): Lammens 4; Mazraoui 4, Yoro 6, Maguire 5 (Heaven 46, 5), Dalot 3 (Dorgu 66, 8); Tielemans 6 (Mount 66, 5), Mainoo 6; Amad 5 (Lacey 66, 7), Fernandes 6, Cunha 7; Mbeumo 5 (Zirkzee 83, 7)

Goals: Fernandes (16 pen), Tielemans (44), Zirkzee (90+2), Dorgu (90+6)

ASTON VILLA (4-2-3-1): Suzuki 6; Harwood-Bellis 5, Lindelof 6, Torres 6, Maatsen 4; Goretzka 6 (Mbaye 78, 6), Kamara 4; Alysson 7 (Barkley 71, 6), McGinn 8 (Hemmings 71, 6), Manzambi 6; Jackson 6 (Madjo 86)

Goals: Alysson (1), Jackson (4), McGinn (48 pen), Goretzka (53)

Here, they twice trailed by two, at the start of both halves. They were 2-0 down after only four minutes and 4-2 behind after 53. Somehow they managed not to lose.

The two goals United scored in added time at the end of the game came from absolutely nowhere. On the touchline Carrick – free from total humiliation for the second time in six days – raised a single finger to the sky.

In the directors' box – where the TV companies seem to have a camera permanently trained these days – fist pumps were in evidence from those in United blazers.

This was another uncomfortable afternoon for Messrs Ratcliffe, Wilcox and Berrada. A collection of new banners in the crowd made feelings known.

For now, Carrick is being spared any of the blame. That is strange and wrong but tells us much about the confused nature of the modern United. If they blame one of their own then they really do have nowhere left to go in search of self-respect.

United's opening against Roma last week had been traumatic enough. This was even worse but the root of it was desperately familiar – incompetent football.

The hapless United full-back Diogo Dalot was to blame for the first goal as Villa's Brazilian Alysson blasted his shot through the Portuguese's limp challenge. Later – after conceding a second half penalty – Dalot was booed off by his own fans.

That was painful and unedifying but completely understandable. Dalot has played more than 250 games for United yet has never even been good enough.

Aston Villa took the lead after just 59 seconds when Alysson (centre) scored at Old Trafford

Nicolas Jackson (centre) doubled the visitors' lead on four minutes as they made a dream start

Bruno Fernandes pulled one back for the hosts with a penalty on 16 minutes in a crazy start

For goal two, goalkeeper Senne Lammens was culpable as he pushed Alysson's shot into the path of Nicolas Jackson.

Once again United were in sink or swim territory. Once again, amazingly, they kept their heads above water – at least for a while.

Boubacar Kamara's 13th minute challenge on Matheus Cunha was stupid as the Brazilian was heading away from goal. But the Bruno Fernandes penalty that followed was calm and precise and the shot of belief United needed was theirs.

For the next 20 minutes or so, Carrick's team played well off the back of it. It was the only part of their game that will look good on the highlights. The United front four pressed and harried the Villa defence to the extent that Unai Emery's team couldn't get up the field.

Villa keeper Zion Suzuki saved well from a Cunha curler while subsequent shots from Fernandes, Kobbie Mainoo and Amad Diallo took deflections and narrowly missed the target.

At some stage it felt as though United's energy must drop. Over time it did. But then they equalised.

Moments after seeing Suzuki save one of his own efforts, Amad found space to pull the ball back for Youri Tielemans who placed his first United goal in from 18 yards against his old club. Carrick's team deserved to be level at that stage and had played some enterprising football to get there. But their self-harm gene surfaced again before long.

Youri Tielemans (right) brought United level before half-time with this strike vs his old club

John McGinn put Villa ahead again just minutes after half-time from the penalty spot

Leon Goretkza (left) made it 4-2 to Villa with this close-range strike as they looked set to win

Joshua Zirkzee (left) pulled one back in second-half stoppage-time with this finish for United

Alysson had almost scored with the last big moment of the first half and in the 47th minute he drew an utterly dumb foul from Dalot and John McGinn scored the penalty firmly.

United are beyond compare in the Premier League when it comes to this kind of thing. Who else in the top flight concedes the kind of goals they do? A case in point came in the 53rd minute.

McGinn's free-kick was heading across goal and away when United defender Leny Yoro inexplicably headed it towards his own goal. Lammens somehow touched the ball on to the bar but when it dropped Leon Goretzka poked it in.

That was the 16th goal United had conceded in the last five games and this time the attempt at a fightback was more muted.

There were some half chances – shots from Cunha and Fernandes – and some territory in which to play.

But this was a game Villa were now able to control rather better and they looked set to ease home until Patrick Dorgu crossed for Joshua Zirkzee to ease a goal in from out of nothing in the 2nd minute of added time.

Old Trafford was emptying fast by the time Dorgu headed in a 96th equaliser from a corner. But by then the collective blood was up once more and it was enough.

Villa appealed for a foul on goalkeeper Suzuki and it's true Lammens had gone looking for him. Was the contact between the two 'keepers significant enough to warrant VAR intervention? Probably not. United had their draw and they had a point.

If the season ended today, they would be down. Yet they were cheered off. Some things you just can't make up.

Fulham star Rodrigo Muniz opened up on his personal agony after scoring on Saturday

Muniz revealed his wife Jady suffered a miscarriage but they are now pregnant again

Muniz has not been afforded a start by new Fulham boss Alvaro Arbeloa, making all eight of his Premier League appearances off the bench so far this season.

#2120. Celtic fans disgracefully boo during minute's silence on Remembrance Sunday: Referee ends tribute after just 13 SECONDS - even after warning from the PA announcer

By LUKE AUGUSTUS, ASSISTANT SPORTS EDITOR

Published: 03:28 EST, 9 November 2026 | Updated: 04:51 EST, 9 November 2026

Celtic were forced to curtail plans to commemorate Remembrance Day after a section of their supporters booed during the planned minute's silence on Remembrance Sunday.

The Bhoys hosted Kilmarnock in the Scottish Premiership on Sunday - with Liam Scales' goal proving the difference in a 1-0 win.

However, ahead of kick-off at Parkhead, the annual tribute to the armed forces and their families was overshadowed by some fans' loud jeers.

As the players from both teams gathered around the centre-circle, an announcement on the public address system said: 'We will have a minute's silence today on Remembrance Sunday to remember all victims of all conflicts. We remember, in particular, the men who served Celtic football club and lost their lives at wartime.'

The audible disdain resulted in referee John Beaton being forced to cut the silence after just 13 seconds.

The noise was so palpable that the Celtic TV broadcast turned down the volume to try and minimise its impact too.

The minute's silence ahead of Celtic versus Kilmarnock was cut short due to some fans booing

Celtic TV turned down the volume to try and minimise the jeers during the curtailed silence

Referee John Beaton (right) cut the minute's silence after 13 seconds as a result of the booing

Furthermore, the lower tier of the standing section was empty until the Green Brigade and other fans started to file in from the concourse when referee Beaton’s whistle signalled the end of the tribute.

Football clubs across the United Kingdom observe a minute's silence with their nearest home fixture around Remembrance Sunday and Armistice Day - with the latter on November 11.

Many teams also wear poppies, something Kilmarnock wore on their strip, but Celtic didn't.

Some Celtic supporters refuse to partake in the tradition or actively take a stance against it, often citing the club’s Irish roots and linking it to the British Army‘s role in The Troubles.

Last season, in a quirk, Celtic hosted Kilmarnock on Remembrance Sunday too - with that minute's silence also cut short due to jeering. That time referee Don Robertson cut it short at 15 seconds.

As well as the booing in that match last term, some fans turned their back on the minute's silence and also made gestures.

Sunday's 1-0 win over Kilmarnock sees Celtic regain their four-point lead at the top of the Scottish Premiership.

It was under-pressure Martin O'Neill's side's first league win in their last three games.

Scales scored the only goal of the game on 26 minutes when he hooked a Camilo Duran cross into the top corner.

Celtic fans show their displeasure during the minute's silence for Remembrance Day in 2025

Celtic - and Kasper Hogh in particular - otherwise had a frustrating day in front of goal, with Benjamin Nygren missing a second-half penalty.

Hogh had an early goal disallowed as his barren run without a goal hit the 16-game mark since he netted a hat-trick at Kilmarnock on his second appearance.

Celtic have 31 points from their opening 13 matches - four more than bitter rivals Rangers who sit second but have a game in hand.

Kilmarnock remain rooted to the bottom of the table with nine points from 13 games - two adrift of 11th-place Aberdeen who have played a match fewer.

#2119. Haaland è una macchina: 66 gol in 60 gare con la Norvegia. I suoi numeri fuori dal mondo

Erling Haaland non si ferma più. La Norvegia supera il Galles per 3-1 nella quinta giornata della Nations League e il suo centravanti aggiunge un'altra rete a una collezione di numeri impressionanti. Dopo un primo tempo senza gol, sono stati Antonio Nusa al 59' e Andreas Schjelderup al 68' a indirizzare la partita. I gallesi hanno provato a riaprirla con David Brooks al 79', ma nel finale è arrivata la firma del solito Haaland, che all'88' ha trasformato un calcio di rigore, chiudendo definitivamente i conti all'Ullevaal Stadion di Oslo.

Con questo gol, il numero 9 del Manchester City raggiunge 66 reti in 60 presenze con la Nazionale norvegese. Una media di 1,1 gol a partita, impressionante anche considerando il livello delle avversarie affrontate nel corso della sua carriera internazionale. Il classe 2000 aveva già conquistato il primato di miglior marcatore nella storia della Norvegia, superando nell'ottobre 2024 il precedente record di Jørgen Juve. Da allora ha continuato ad allargare il divario, trasformando un primato storico in un'impresa sempre più difficile da avvicinare.

A soli 26 anni, Haaland viaggia a una velocità realizzativa impressionante e continua a confermarsi uno degli attaccanti più prolifici del calcio mondiale. Non soltanto un bomber al Manchester City, ma anche il simbolo di una Nazionale che sogna di tornare protagonista nelle grandi competizioni internazionali. Sessantasei gol in sessanta partite, del resto, sono numeri da macchina.

Luca Toni, l'attaccante italiano che più gli somiglia, lo incorona nell’intervista concessa oggi a La Repubblica: «Pio Esposito parla coi gol, ma mi piacciono anche la sua umiltà, il suo carattere e la sua voglia di migliorare. È forte, in tutti i sensi».

#2114. Il sosia di Ronaldinho inganna Ravenna. Parlano Mancini e Scalvini: le top news delle 22

Matteo Dagasso si prende la scena in Francia: il centrocampista del Venezia è partito titolare nel 2-1 con cui l’Italia Under 21 ha battuto ieri a Laval i pari età transalpini e ha servito l’assist per il vantaggio di Francesco Camarda, dopo lo stop causato da alcuni fastidi al ginocchio. A TuttoMercatoWeb.com ha parlato il suo procuratore Carlo Di Renzo: "Il suo spazio lo troverà, ma non solo nel Venezia. Anche se dovesse andare in altri club. Ha una testa da top player e tecnicamente è un giocatore che ha entrambe le fasi di gioco e dà equilibrio a tutto il reparto”.

Si avvicina a grandi passi il debutto di Ronaldinho con la maglia del Ravenna, domani alle 17.30 contro il Forlì. Un evento che non solo ha scatenato l'entusiasmo dei sostenitori ravennati, ma ha anche avvicinato a questa partita curiosi non solitamente vicino al mondo del calcio, televisioni italiane ed estere ed esponenti del pallone che in qualche modo hanno avuto a che fare con l'ex giocatore - tra le altre - di Barcellona e Milan. Un tumulto generale che causa anche eventi piuttosto bizzarri. Come riporta LaCasadC.com, nei pressi dello store del Ravenna si è presentato Ronaldinho: o almeno questo è quello che credevano tutti quei tifosi che lo hanno applaudito e hanno sperato di strappare un selfie con lui. In realtà, si trattava di un fake. Episodio che la dice lunga di come l'arrivo nella città romagnola del fuoriclasse brasiliano abbia fatto anche perdere il lume della ragione alla gente.

Gli occhi della Lazio e dello Sporting Braga sul capitano dell'Under 19 tedesca. Secondo quanto raccolto da TMW, queste due società sono sulle tracce del centrocampista difensivo classe 2008 Ebu Bekir Is. Di proprietà dell'Eintracht Francoforte, club che ha un'opzione per rinnovargli per un'altra stagione l'attuale accordo in scadenza a giugno, Is è attualmente in forza alla seconda squadra tedesca. Però da giovanissimo - quasi due anni fa - ha già fatto il suo esordio in prima squadra, in una sfida di Europa League contro la Roma vinta 2-0 dalla squadra della Capitale.

Il ct azzurro Roberto Mancini parla in conferenza stampa introducendo l'ultima partita del girone di Nations League cui è attesa l'Italia, sul campo del Belgio. Di seguito alcune delle sue dichiarazioni: "Pensavamo di recuperare Cambiaghi, abbiamo provato stamani, ma aveva dolore. Sicuramente 2 cambi li faremo (l'altro è Frattesi, ndr), ma dobbiamo valutare chi ha bisogno di riposare. Quella di proporre Dimarco alto è un'opzione che abbiamo pensato da tempo, lo decideremo domani, le cose vanno viste in base alla partita e anche durante. Calafiori? Lui è da valutare".

Giorgio Scalvini, difensore dell'Atalanta e dell'Italia, è intervenuto ai microfoni di Sky Sport alla vigilia del match contro il Belgio: "Domani giocheremo al massimo con l'atteggiamento giusto". Tra i migliori nella partita di San Siro, il classe 2003 risponde così quando gli viene chiesto se sono pronti a fare lo switch dopo il 3-1 alla Francia: "Non è facile, ma una volta finita una partita bisogna cercare subito di recuperare le energie. Abbiamo avuto pochi giorni, ma non è un alibi, domani cercheremo di fare il massimo".

Dopo le iniziali preoccupazioni dovute alle dichiarazioni di Tuchel, l'Inter e soprattutto i tifosi nerazzurri possono tirare un sospiro di sollievo. Come riportato da tutti i media locali, Djed Spence oggi è tornato ad allenarsi con il resto del gruppo e sarà a disposizione dell'Inghilterra per il match di Nations League contro la Spagna. Tutto come previsto dunque, il problema muscolare è stato smaltito nel giro di qualche giorno.

#2111. Some are calling this 'Tuchel 2.0' and his latest squad does feel like that. Here's why England think a tilt at the European Championship is within reach, writes IAN LADYMAN

Published: 17:35 EST, 6 November 2026 | Updated: 05:45 EST, 9 November 2026

One of Thomas Tuchel’s favourite analogies when it comes to the development of his England team is that of a train leaving a station with only the chosen few on it. As time moves on, the bodies lying on the tracks are growing greater in number.

Consider this statistic. Of the 26 players who travelled to the World Cup in America 13 are not in the squad chosen by England manager Tuchel for next week’s Nations League games against Croatia and Spain. And that does not include Cole Palmer and Phil Foden who, after missing out in the summer, were expected by many of us to have forced a way back in by now.

Tuchel’s latest selection has been affected by injuries, of course. But more significant is that the experienced German believes he is now starting to see an England team and squad develop in a way and in a direction that even he hadn’t envisaged once Argentina ended his World Cup hopes in that dismal Atlanta semi-final in July.

Different feelings, different style of play and, to a degree, different players.

Some are calling this ‘Tuchel 2.0’ and it does feel a little bit like that. The start of the second phase of Tuchel’s tenure, the start of the road towards a tilt at another trophy, the European Championships of 2028.

Having admitted to some worry ahead of the four-game international break FIFA saddled us all with last month, Tuchel sat down yesterday looking and sounding like a man once again certain of himself.

Tuchel sat down with the media looking and sounding like a man once again certain of himself

The inclusion of Jobe Bellingham — Jude’s younger brother (right) — was the headline act but there were other messages written within

Certain enough of a direct, quick, athletic style of play that helped England to two wins over Czech Republic and one – by seven goals – against Croatia in Rijeka. And certain that a dependence on players like Jordan Henderson, Dan Burn and John Stones for what we may politely call ‘squad culture management’ lies now in the past.

All of this may crash down in Madrid next Sunday. Spain have already beaten Tuchel’s England once – at Wembley in late September – and a repeat this month would ask familiar questions about England against the really good teams.

But for now, Tuchel is confident enough to claim that he has found his path.

‘We said at some point we need to take the next step, to build on what we have, to get better and to improve otherwise you’re just left behind,’ said Tuchel yesterday.

‘That’s how football works. You have been champion in one season but you need to improve, you need to bring in new players for competition, young players, new energy, fresh energy. It’s also the way, naturally, the national team goes.

‘We cannot be stuck in the same personnel and in the same style of play.

‘I agreed with you last month. I was the first to admit I was not 100 per cent happy with the pure level of football we played in the US. I saw a higher level in training [than on the field], I saw a higher level in glimpses.

‘We adjusted some things tactically, created some new positions and took the next steps.’

The Tuchel we sat down with in late September still wore the scars of the summer’s disappointment – and the criticism that came with it – rather visibly. It was the first time we had seen him like that in his two years in the job. He was tetchy and easily provoked. Call it the Argentina effect if you like.

Here, he appeared rather healed. Good results tend to do that for a manager and a little time helps too. If he believes his team has moved on then maybe he has also and he had to.

Certainly, his selection squad speaks to that. The inclusion of Jobe Bellingham – Jude’s younger brother – was the headline act but there were other messages written within.

Henderson and Burn are available but not included. So too Arsenal’s Noni Madueke, excluded to the benefit of the more direct Liverpool winger Rio Ngumoha.

‘We need runners who will go between the lines,’ said Tuchel. A clear indication, there, that too much of England’s summer wing play was too slow and too conservatively narrow.

Palmer, Foden and Kobbie Mainoo are also on the outside looking in once again and the case of the Manchester United midfielder is perhaps indicative of how Tuchel now operates.

Moved to apologise to him in front of the whole squad in July for his lack of minutes at the World Cup, Tuchel was determined Mainoo would play in the opening four games of the Nations League campaign.

‘I was desperate to give him the minutes and desperate to include him in this new start,’ explained Tuchel.

‘But then he pulled out. He missed an excellent camp and also entered a difficult, difficult spell for himself.

‘Man United fell off a cliff a little bit performance-wise. So this is why he now suffers.’

We can talk about the whys and wherefores of that logic. If Mainoo was good enough in September then why is he not good enough now? How desperate could Tuchel really have been to play him that one withdrawal with injury closed the door for the next camp and opened it instead for Bellingham jnr?

This, though, is how Tuchel works. He is an adept tactician but he’s also a mood coach, someone who – after sobering club experiences at places like PSG, Chelsea and Bayern Munich – places huge emphasis on environments and gut feels and culture. If he feels something works, he doesn’t like to pick it apart without good reason.

This is why for example the growing voice and influence of Elliot Anderson has left Jordan Henderson suddenly surplus. Tuchel says the door is not closed on a player who will be 38 by the time the next Euros are played and he called him this week to say so. In Tuchel’s mind, though, England will now move without the veteran and others of his ilk.

‘I was concerned before the last camp that we missed the players who kept our standards,’ said Tuchel.

‘I was worried that we missed so many of them. But then this group found their own way and their own voices.

It’s also reasonable to ask why our head coach can’t find room in his squad – if not his team – for the game changing duo of Palmer and Foden

‘I know what the others did. They built a platform on which these ones can build.’

Time will tell. To a degree, Spain will tell. Can England play the kind of higher tempo, front foot football we saw against Croatia and the Czechs against the world and European champions next Sunday? They embarrassed us when we didn’t have the ball at Wembley.

It’s also reasonable to ask why our head coach can’t find room in his squad – if not his team – for the game-changing duo of Palmer and Foden. England lacked good substitutes at the World Cup. There was too much like for like.

England need to win in order for such decisions to be vindicated and Tuchel knows that.

Asked yesterday if he felt as invigorated as he looked he said he did. ‘The last camp gave me a lot of energy, gave me a lot of extra belief,’ he said.

‘It gave me a lot of optimism to not compromise on the way England teams should play. Be physical but also step up in the pure level of performances.’

It’s certainly time for this now. The summer only served to expose English shortcomings. Tuchel’s train must arrive in Madrid next weekend, not only with fresh personnel but also new ideas clearly apparent.

Read more: Thomas Tuchel admits his relationship with Jude Bellingham is 'up and down' – but on the mend – as he reveals he's apologised to Kobbie Mainoo who is struggling at 'off a cliff' Man United

#2109. FIGC, ammesse le candidature di Galliani e Malagò: Marotta si candida come Consigliere Federale

FIGC, ammesse le candidature di Galliani e Malagò: Marotta si candida come Consigliere Federale

Installa la nostra App gratuita e accedi più velocemente alle notizie

Mentre navighi nell'app, scorri il dito da sinistra a destra dello schermo per tornare alle pagine precedenti

Scegli Fcinter1908.it come tuo sito preferito su Google: clicca qui

Via libera ufficiale alla sfida a due per la Figc. Sono state ammesse le candidature alla presidenza della Federcalcio - in ordine di presentazione - di Giovanni Malagò e Adriano Galliani. Corredate dai rispettivi programmi, le due candidature erano state depositate entro il termine stabilito dal regolamento, ovvero 40 giorni prima dell'assemblea elettiva convocata per lunedì 14 dicembre presso l'Hotel Hilton Rome Airport di Fiumicino.

La candidatura di Malagò è stata sottoscritta dai delegati delle società di Serie A, mentre quella di Galliani è stata sottoscritta dai delegati degli atleti e tecnici.

Sono state inoltre ammesse le seguenti candidature a consigliere federale: in rappresentanza della Lega Calcio Serie A Giorgio Chiellini, Stefano Campoccia, Giuseppe Marotta, Claudio Lotito, Saverio Sticchi Damiani; in rappresentanza della LNP Serie B Antonio Gozzi; in rappresentanza della Lega Pro Giulio Gallazzi; in rappresentanza della L.N.D. #2105. Inside Cristiano Ronaldo Jr's bid to step out of his father's shadow after his Portugal Under-17s call-up: From padel beef with Rio Ferdinand to a Coca-Cola BAN

Published: 02:00 EST, 8 November 2026 | Updated: 05:50 EST, 9 November 2026

He has spent his whole life being measured against European football's greatest goalscorer. Now Cristiano Ronaldo Jr. has taken his biggest step yet.

The 16-year-old has been called up to Portugal's U17 squad for the first time, and will face Andorra on November 11 and Serbia on November 14. And the timing is hard to ignore.

The famous No. 7 shirt Ronaldo Sr wore for two decades will now be vacant after he announced his retirement from international football on Thursday.

It's a heavy shirt for anyone to inherit. Luis Figo graced the number when he led Portugal’s golden generation to the 2006 World Cup semi-finals, and Ronaldo Sr himself carried it when he captained them to the European Championship in 2016. Both won the Ballon d’Or and are two of the most tantalising players the nation has ever produced.

Portugal’s power vacuum is also a pressure point. As the son of the man who wore that shirt, Ronaldo Jr. will attract more attention, and more expectation.

The national team lacks its focal point, and few names carry more weight than his. He is the natural successor in marketing terms, if nothing else. Whether he is the player to take it is another matter.

Cristiano Ronaldo Jr - son of the Portuguese great - is looking to forge his own football career

This week the 16-year-old earned his first call-up to Portugal's U17 team

The weight of his father's expectations has been there from the start. Ronaldo Jr once floated the idea of becoming a goalkeeper, and his father’s reaction was swift. “No, I don’t want that! Are you joking or what?” Ronaldo Sr responds in the 2015 documentary Ronaldo.

Up front for the Juventus academy, his record would make the man chasing 1,000 career goals jealous: a staggering 58 goals in 23 games for the Under-9s. Yet his father was never satisfied. Asked at the 2020 Globe Soccer Awards, with his son sitting in the audience, whether the boy had what it takes to reach the top, Ronaldo Sr’s answer was blunt.

“Not yet,” he said. “I’m hard with him sometimes because he drinks Coca-Cola and Fanta. (He has) chips and fries; I fight with him.”

Hard data on his youth career since then is limited. Reports of his interest in switching to the Real Madrid academy suggested a desire to develop and compete.

Rio Ferdinand, who played alongside Ronaldo Sr. at Manchester United, has seen that competitive streak in the son on the padel court.

“I was in the Red Sea a couple of days ago; I play padel every day. (When) me and Cristiano Jr. get mad, we don't speak for like two days.” He exclaimed on Five Presents. Like his father, he doesn’t take losing well.

His body, though, has settled the question of position. At 6 ft 3 in, he already stands taller than his father. Growth spurts may temporarily cost him some of the pace and the effortless coordination of his youth. A forced adaption of his style is not unusual: Ronaldo’s former teammates Gareth Bale and Scott McTominay were both moved into new positions to begin their professional careers.

It is also a path his father walked. Forget stepovers, flicks and trademark ‘Ronaldo chop’. Jr’s game is currently closer to the Ronaldo of Juventus and the Old Trafford return: a penalty-box predator who lives on timing and movement rather than dribbling past defenders.

The young striker has represented the youth academies of Man United, Real Madrid and Juventus

But his ultra-professional father said his son needs to cut out the Coca-Cola if he wants to make it at the top level

His Portugal numbers are respectable, not remarkable. Five goals in 13 appearances is a decent return, but he has played just 522 minutes, an average of around 40 per game, and scored roughly once every 104 minutes. However, it is a tiny sample size.

To reach the top, he will need the work ethic his father is famous for. “I’ve never seen a more professional person,” Paul Scholes, Ronaldo Sr’s former Manchester United teammate, told Premier League Productions. “He was out there all day long, practising … he had weights on his ankles.”

Habits like that are built in demanding environments, and Jr’s has been a gentle one.

Playing in Saudi Arabia still has its positives. As Jr. transitions from boy to man, weaker opposition has given him room to grow into his body and build confidence as he prepares for men's senior football. But that breathing space has a shelf life.

He has options. He could stay and share a dressing room with his father, or return to one of the academies of Ronaldo Sr’s former clubs, with Real Madrid, Juventus and Manchester United all reported by The Sun to be interested. He has the name, the call-up and the options, but not yet the proof.

When Ronaldo Sr walked away from Portugal, he left behind a shirt, a record and a question. Saudi Arabia cannot give his son the answer. If he wants international caps and Champions League football, he must leave Al Nassr and his old man for a stage where nobody gives him an inch.

The surname opened the door. Only he can walk through it.

#2104. Bellinazzo: "Inter post Suning modello di gestione virtuosa. Napoli? Prima fiammate e poi..."

Intervenuto ai microfoni di TMW, Marco Bellinazzo, giornalista esperto di finanza ed economia applicata al calcio, ha citato anche l'Inter come esempio di modello virtuoso:

"È freschissimo il bilancio dell'Atalanta, appena approvato con l'undicesimo utile consecutivo. E l'Atalanta è un club che magari è più vicino alla dimensione della Fiorentina, o perlomeno lo era fino a qualche anno fa. L'Atalanta, oltre ad aver fatto undici utili consecutivi, ha fatto anche il record storico con 325 milioni di ricavi. E non a caso ha realizzato una novantina di milioni di plusvalenze, credo.

Questo dà l'idea di quanto sia ormai connesso in maniera strettissima quello che avviene in campo con quello che avviene fuori dal campo. Una sana e corretta gestione tecnica, che ti fa vincere, che attraverso il settore tecnico e giovanile ti fa creare giocatori di livello, ti permette anche poi di strutturare al meglio la società. Magari investendo, costruendo un centro sportivo, costruendo uno stadio e aumentando i ricavi. L'Atalanta, e in parte anche l'Inter post Suning, hanno realizzato questo tipo di gestione virtuosa.

Anche il Napoli di De Laurentiis, per alcuni anni. Però, per esempio, il Napoli è proprio la dimostrazione di come, se tu non riesci a far fruttare le vittorie sportive investendo in strutture, nello stadio, in un centro sportivo di qualità, nel vivaio, poi quei successi ti possono dare delle fiammate e possono sostenere i conti, ma se non hanno un seguito poi ne risenti in maniera consistente. Tant'è che il Napoli si è ritrovato a non poter fare mercato, o a scegliere di non fare mercato quest'estate, ma è stata una scelta obbligata dal fair play finanziario".

#2099. Forest Green apologise for failing to commemorate Remembrance Day at a home match due to 'mistake'

By LUKE AUGUSTUS, ASSISTANT SPORTS EDITOR

Published: 10:15 EST, 8 November 2026 | Updated: 07:03 EST, 9 November 2026

Forest Green Rovers have apologised for failing to commemorate Remembrance Day at a home game, labelling it as 'human error' and a 'mistake' from the club.

Remembrance Day is November 11 with Forest Green's sole fixture around that date having been Saturday's 2-0 FA Cup first-round defeat at Luton Town. Both teams commemorated the occasion ahead of kick-off at Kenilworth Road.

Forest Green's last home fixture prior to that was a 2-2 draw with Harrogate Town on October 31 in the National League.

That encounter at The New Lawn saw supporters of the fifth-tier side take to a fan forum post-match questioning why nothing was in place for Remembrance Day - due to their then-forthcoming clash with Luton being an away tie.

Forest Green CEO Dane Vince replied on the club's Fan Advisory Board, admitting it was an oversight on their part.

'The lack of Remembrance at a home game is simply down to a human error/mistake on the club,' he wrote.

Forest Green Rovers have apologised for not commemorating Remembrance Day at a home game (National League side pictured doing so during Saturday's FA Cup 2-0 defeat at Luton)

Forest Green failed to do so in their home draw vs Harrogate on October 31, calling it a 'mistake'

Vince continued that a condensed schedule in this period didn't help matters, but still acknowledged that the club should have done more.

'It didn't help that we had a sizable number of games in a small time frame (including the FA Cup replay) beforehand; albeit that isn't an excuse, we should have got this right.'

Following the incident, Vince confirmed that Forest Green would contact the Royal British Legion for advice on future commemorations to ensure this wouldn't happen again. He also underlined that the missed home commemoration did not signal any change in the club's support for Remembrance or the Royal British Legion.

'This was a simple error/mistake,' he reiterated.

#2096. Mancini chiude il caso rigore con Esposito: "Nulla di grave, cose che succedono spesso"

Roberto Mancini, commissario tecnico dell'Italia, ha parlato in conferenza dalla sala stampa dello stadio Re Baldovino di Bruxelles, dove domani gli Azzurri saranno ospiti del Belgio in Nations League: "Bello che siamo qua a giocarci la qualificazione ai quarti. La partita sarà complicata, il Belgio non è così lontano dalla Francia, al mondiale è andato fuori all'ultimo minuto. Non sarà facile, ma possiamo anche chiudere il girone al primo posto".

Ci dà qualche indicazione di formazione? "Ho le idee chiare, dipende dalle condizioni dei ragazzi".

Ci dice come stanno? "Pensavamo di recuperare Cambiaghi, abbiamo provato stamani, ma aveva dolore. Sicuramente 2 cambi li faremo (l'altro è Frattesi, ndr), ma dobbiamo valutare chi ha bisogno di riposare".

Ha alternato sempre Calafiori. Ha bisogno di rifiatare? Dimarco può giocare più avanti in pianta più stabile? "Sì, dovremo valutarlo domani. Quella di Dimarco è un'opzione che abbiamo pensato da tempo, lo decideremo domani, le cose vanno viste in base alla partita e anche durante".

State vincendo anche per Malagò? Mi spiego meglio, come se voleste dire: "Non rompete il giochino, stiamo andando bene". "Ci teniamo per tutto (ride, ndr). Siamo qua per vincere e per cercare di qualificarci, è il nostro obiettivo di domani sera".

Il suo secondo ciclo è iniziato con una sconfitta proprio contro il Belgio. "Io avevo sensazioni positive già dopo l’esordio all’Olimpico, perché nel secondo tempo avevo visto buone cose: a un certo punto avremmo anche meritato il pareggio. Ora però voglio guardare avanti: faremo la nostra partita come sempre, conoscendo la qualità dell’avversario che è tecnico e veloce. Non si discosta molto dalla Francia per livello. Di sicuro è bello essere qui a competere per la qualificazione, anche con un piccolo in vantaggio in classifica. Dobbiamo essere orgogliosi di aver conquistato 10 punti. Siamo in testa a un girone pieno di squadre forti, pur sapendo che dobbiamo ancora migliorare. Non è una cosa da poco".

Che dice a Van Bommel, convinto che inconsciamente il fatto di avere 2 risultati su 3 potrebbe togliervi qualcosa? "Non gestiremo il vantaggio. Non fa parte della nostra mentalità stare lì ad aspettare, anche perché il rischio è che in un attimo gli equilibri cambino e non si riesca poi a recuperare".

Perché l'Italia non ha più paura? "Noi giochiamo sempre con l’idea di vincere, perché è il coraggio che ti porta a farlo. Il calcio è un gioco divertente, ma la gente è contenta solo quando arriva il risultato. Il vantaggio semmai va sfruttato".

Ci racconta della discussione tra lei e gli Esposito per il rigore non calciato da Pio e lasciato al fratello Sebastiano? "Non è successo niente di grave. Queste cose nel calcio capitano spesso".
