#2093. Xabi Alonso was brought in to end the chaos at Chelsea and he is failing. PLUS: Football's most vanilla pundit gives out-of-his-depth Michael Carrick an easy ride: IAN LADYMAN on My Football Weekend

Published: 07:00 EST, 9 November 2026 | Updated: 07:55 EST, 9 November 2026

The whiff of Premier League failure is in the air as the smoke clears from Bonfire Night.

It always feels this way by the time we get to the November international break.

Managers and their players have had time to settle in and teething problems no longer cut it as a reason for autumn struggles.

Worst of all, Christmas is coming and we all know what tends to happen to teams who are struggling as the end of the year nears.

So at places like Manchester United, Crystal Palace, Sunderland, Newcastle and Spurs there is anxiety about expectations not being met.

Xabi Alonso was hired to break the cycle at Chelsea and he is not yet succeeding

Xabi Alonso's side would appear to be the team that is failing without anybody seeming to notice.

At Stamford Bridge, the environment is conducive to Alonso and his team doing well this season.

Chelsea – not in Europe after last season's troubles – are only playing once a week. So Alonso – hired for his coaching reputation – has time on the grass with players who should arrive every weekend with freshness in their legs.

It's the environment that enabled Michael Carrick to fool everyone at Manchester United last season. While the rest of the Premier League got bogged down in the schedule, United sailed upwards on the back of a lighter load.

When we look at Alonso's team we see only familiar Chelsea things – and not in a good way.

Inconsistent tactics, some strange selections and, on the back of it, some very ordinary results.

This is the cycle Alonso was hired to break and he is not yet succeeding.

Sunday's defeat to Sunderland can be added to league setbacks against Spurs, Brentford and Arsenal.

They were unimpressive in beating United a couple of weeks ago also. Carrick's team outplayed them in the first half and should have been two or three goals ahead.

Under Alonso, Chelsea are yet to establish an identity or discernible patterns. It is hard to know yet what his best back four (or five) looks like. In the absence of the injured striker Joao Pedro, Alonso seems happier to play without one rather than use Danny Welbeck, signed for just this scenario in the summer.

The absence of Joao Pedro is significant while Alonso is also without his first choice goalkeeper Emi Martinez. These things matter.

But Chelsea have a huge squad of international players. Maybe it's too big. Whatever the case Alonso must get the best out of it soon.

Nobody seems to be talking about how very underwhelming the whole thing feels. But if it continues, they soon will be.

On Match of the Day on Sunday night, football's most vanilla pundit Dion Dublin was asked whether Michael Carrick has done enough to keep his job.

'I think so,' said Dublin. 'I think Michael has all the tools but the players just aren't doing what they need to do.'

At every other club in the Premier League, under-performing players and the bad results that tend to follow lead to intense scrutiny and criticism of the manager. It's his job to affect these things.

Not at United, though, where the level of public and media support for Carrick remains extraordinary and illogical.

If we applied this level of leeway to everybody else then nobody would ever be sacked.

Dion Dublin, pictured on Homes Under The Hammer duty, gave Michael Carrick an easy ride

Carrick is out of his depth at sinking Manchester United - his team are a shambles

Yes, there are a number of factors at play at Old Trafford. Yes, we know what they are and who is to blame for them. Yes, it's a very difficult job.

But the truth is that Carrick is failing in a quite spectacular fashion.

Of all the managers to have followed Sir Alex Ferguson over the last 13 and a half years, Erik ten Hag was the one who looked most out of his depth in terms of the chaotic nature of his team's football.

Carrick is now on the way to eclipsing that. His team are a shambles and that's on him. Once again, this is a mediocre league and it's quite criminal that Carrick has his group of players in the bottom three.

The former Middlesbrough manager is so out of his depth it's quite painful to watch.

It was another extraordinary afternoon at Old Trafford as United drew 4-4 with Aston Villa.

Unai Emery's Villa team is going through yet another regeneration after the sale of big-name players in the summer and balancing the Champions League with domestic football will once again be a challenge.

Emery seems to have made his mind up about one of his squad members, though, after former United and Chelsea winger Alejandro Garnacho was left out of the squad entirely yesterday.

Garnacho is a talented player but has made only 14 Premier League starts – and none at Villa – since leaving Old Trafford for Chelsea in the summer of 2025.

The Argentine is still only 22 so time is on his side but at some point he is going to have to do something on the field to justify the investment in a career that is currently going nowhere.

Alejandro Garnacho was left out of the squad entirely yesterday by Aston Villa

Sky's excellent touchline reporter Patrick Davison had his work cut out post-match at Old Trafford as he interviewed one manager in danger of the sack and another who had just flounced down the tunnel like a chap with his pants on fire.

Neither Michael Carrick nor Unai Emery seemed particularly comfortable in the face of Davison's questions and that's exactly as it should be.

He is there to ask questions on behalf of a broadcaster that ploughs billions of pounds into our national sport each year. He is not there to apply soothing balm.

There would appear to be many others happy to do that anyway.

Sky's Patrick Davison (right) is not there to apply soothing balm with his questions

Less impressive was the appearance of Sky's latest metric that appeared on screen during Nottingham Forest's dismantling of Manchester City on Saturday.

Titled simply 'pressures' it indicated that Forest were doing rather better at it than City.

But what did it mean? Does anybody know? Or indeed care?

Two seasons ago at the City Ground I saw local boy Ryan Yates give Casemiro such a chasing as Forest beat Manchester United that the great Brazilian was taken off before he was sent off.

On Saturday by the river, Yates made his first league start of the season as Oliver Glasner’s team overwhelmed City.

Beforehand Yates gave an address to his team-mates. He told them about what it meant to play for the club and touched briefly on its history.

He told me once in an interview that he tends to tell all new arrivals at Forest what the two stars on the shirt actually mean – two European Cups won in 1979 and 1980.

It’s smart work by a lad who knows what football means in Nottingham and it was smart work by his manager who knew that his team needed an injection of something visceral to drag him from a trough of failure that was threatening to cost him his job.

Manchester United could probably do with a bit of that right now. So, possibly, could Chelsea and indeed Tottenham. At Liverpool, they lament the absence from the team of Trent Alexander-Arnold and Curtis Jones.

It takes an awful lot to make up a Premier League team these days. Place of birth is not a priority any more and that’s understandable. But sometimes it helps.

When was the last time an English team won the title with a poor goalkeeper?

Flick back over the last couple of decades and it's hard to find an example.

So what hope for Manchester City this season?

Gianluigi Donnarumma is anything but a poor goalkeeper but the problem is that he is playing like one and it is there that City's current problems start.

Enzo Maresca's back four looks shot to pieces and that often happens when they lose faith in the chap behind them.

Maresca says he won't change. 'My team is Gigi plus ten others,' is his take.

Enzo Maresca says he will not change goalkeeper despite Gianluigi Donnarumma's problems

His back up is 34-year-old Argentinean Geronimo Rulli, a member of his country's World Cup squad with nine caps to his name.

Rulli is well-travelled and has some pedigree. He was, for example, Villarreal's hero as they beat Manchester United in a Europa League final shoot out in 2021.

If Maresca trusts him then maybe now is the time to show it. If he doesn't then why did City sign him?

Liverpool goalkeeper Alisson is perhaps not quite what he once was at the age of 34 but produced a throwback performance for his team at Crystal Palace. Without him, Liverpool would not have won.

Nevertheless Palace's struggles go on and their coach Pierre Sage is in danger of losing his job.

However the south London club cannot say they were not warned about trouble ahead.

Former manager Oliver Glasner spent most of last season in a huff about what he saw as a failure by the Palace board to invest properly in the squad.

Palace have won twice in ten Premier League games this season and it seems Glasner may have been right.

Brighton continue to fly high and to produce such an energetic performance to beat Brentford having played away at Getafe in Europe on Thursday was testimony to the fitness of Fabian Hurzeler's squad.

The Seagulls were certainly helped by having to play against ten for an hour, though, and on Match of the Day the enduringly listenable Jonathan Pearce spoke for all of us as he watched Chema Andres go down after minimal contact to his cheek from Brentford's Igor Thiago.

'That was nothing, nothing, nothing,' said Pearce as Thiago walked off.

Coming so soon after theatrics from Alexis MacAllister and Gabriel in the Liverpool-Arsenal game last week, the issue of players exaggerating facial contact to get opponents sent off remains a live one.

Brentford's manager Keith Andrews was measured on the issue but suggested the tactic was becoming 'a game within a game'.

It's a problem and referees head Howard Webb needs his officials to get a grip of it.

Brighton's Pascal Gross has now created more than 25 chances for team-mates this season and that's more than anyone else in the Premier League.

Gross cost £1.2million when he returned to the club from Borussia Dortmund last January.

It constitutes another steal in the market by Brighton's stat boffins but this time they probably didn't need the data to convince them of its merits.

Pascal Gross scored on Sunday and has now created more than 25 chances for team-mates

Bournemouth, meanwhile, cast their net as widely as anybody these days and that includes all the way down the English pyramid.

The South Coast club – on an eight-match winning streak now under Marco Rose – have a relationship with yesterday's FA Cup giant killers Wimborne from the seventh tier.

Wimborne of the Southern League Premier South beat League Two Crawley in Round One and will this week send their Chilean teenage striker Angel Diaz for a trial at the Premier League club.

Story of last season in the EFL was probably Lincoln City's promotion to the Championship where, after 15 games, they are just about keeping their heads above water in 21st place.

Architect of that incredible rise was former futsal coach Michael Skubala who rather sullied the romantic feel of it all by quitting almost immediately to join Bristol City.

This weekend Skubala's team were thrashed 5-0 at home by Norwich with the home fans singing that their manager would be 'sacked in the morning'.

Lincoln have already won at Ashton Gate, back in September, at the start of a run that has seen City win just two from ten.

Sometimes, it really does pay just to stay where you are.

#2092. Union Brescia, Corini: "Era importante fare il nostro, il Cittadella ha una gara non facile"

Eugenio Corini, tecnico dell'Union Brescia, ha commentato così la vittoria contro l'Albinoleffe con relativo aggancio in vetta al Cittadella: "Venivamo da una settimana oggettivamente molto complicata, perché abbiamo avuto un paio di situazioni da gestire durante gli allenamenti e devo ringraziare lo staff sanitario. Un paio di giocatori hanno giocato pur non essendo al cento per cento. Nel primo quarto d'ora abbiamo fatto fatica, contro un avversario di tutto rispetto, che sa giocare e ha profondità tecnica. Poi siamo usciti fuori noi e, nel secondo tempo, abbiamo sofferto il minimo sindacale, coprendo molto bene gli spazi. Devo fare una menzione speciale per Rizzo perché ha giocato una partita di grande qualità in un ruolo non del tutto suo".

Sulle prestazioni di Marras e Balestrero: "Ci sono giocatori - riporta TuttoC.com - che entrano nel mirino della critica e si fa fatica a capire il perché. La resilienza e la volontà di andare oltre le difficoltà certificano il valore della squadra. Balestrero ci ha creduto alla fine, lui è il nostro capitano ed è un giocatore indiscutibile sul piano umano e tecnico. Marras è un altro ragazzo che meritava il gol. Di Donato, la scorsa settimana, ha detto che siamo una squadra umile e questo è il complimento più bello che potessimo ricevere".

Il Brescia prosegue la sua rincorsa: "Il campionato è lungo, abbiamo reagito al momento di difficoltà, quando la vetta era molto distante, ma la stagione è ancora molto lunga. Adesso dobbiamo prepararci bene, voglio una squadra sempre incazzata e con un alto livello di competizione, sia da parte di chi gioca dall'inizio, sia da parte di chi entra. Oggi ci godiamo la vittoria ma sappiamo che col Renate ci aspetta un'altra partita difficilissima. Spero di avere una grande cornice di pubblico, abbiamo bisogno della spinta dei nostri tifosi per il momento che stiamo attraversando. Il dubbio sul gol di Balestrero? Un po' si teme sempre quando c'è una revisione, ma dal campo mi sembrava una rete regolarissima e fortunatamente così è stato".

Domani il Cittadella se la vedrà con la Folgore Caratese: "Era importante vincere e fare il nostro, ci abbiamo creduto fino alla fine ed è un aspetto fondamentale. Siamo primi con una partita in più, ora tocca al Cittadella che domani è atteso da una partita non semplicissima".

#2090. Former EFL player highlights ‘worry’ about QPR’s promotion chances

QPR are sat in 8th place in the Championship table after 15 fixtures ahead of West Ham and Sheffield United. They are in the play-offs along with Stoke City, Wolves, West Brom, Swansea City and Southampton.

The Hoops won 2-1 at home against Lincoln City last time out before the international break after goals by Richard Kone and Glen Kamara. They are back in action next weekend against Millwall at The Den.

Parkin, who played for the likes of Ipswich Town, Luton Town and Swindon Town, has delivered his verdict on the London outfit on the Benjamin Bloom YouTube channel: “If you offered the top eight to any QPR fan right now they’d snap your hand off. It’s been a terrifically positive start to the season. I think I’ve been 100% positive about Julien Stephan throughout.”

He added: “There’s more to come from some of the forward players. I still do worry if Kone was to get injured again and (Rumarn) Burrell misses games this season, is there enough depth in the forward positions to get the goals and get QPR to where they want to be.”

QPR swooped to sign highly-rated goalkeeper Pierce Charles on loan from Manchester City in the summer. He is excellent with the ball at his feet and the former Sheffield Wednesday man has been a useful asset for the Hoops in this campaign to date.

In defence, the R’s have some solid operators like Jimmy Dunne and Ronnie Edwards. However, only Middlesbrough have conceded more than them in the top eight this term so they could do with tightening things up at the back a little bit.

Kamara has turned into a shrewd addition following his arrival in the last transfer window. He battles it out with the likes of Jonathan Varane and Nicolas Madsen for a place in Julien Stephan‘s starting XI in the middle of the park.

Further up the pitch, QPR have a host of dangerous wingers like Harvey Vale, Koki Saito, Kwame Poku and Paul Smyth. Kone has been in decent form over recent times, with Rumarn Burrell still out injured.

As Parkin has alluded to, QPR could struggle for goals if Kone joined Burrell in the treatment room. They don’t have too much competition and depth in their position at this moment in time.

Therefore, the Hoops should look to sign a new striker in the January transfer window. They will get the chance to bolster their attacking arsenal this winter by landing another option.

The R’s will also have the opportunity to let some players head out the exit door as well if needed. That would help free up space and funds in their squad to pave the way for reinforcements.

QPR will be in confident mood after their win over Lincoln City. They will be hoping for more of the same against Millwall next up.

#2087. Declan Rice reveals he's stepped back from social media because it can 'ruin your whole day' after sick trolling of him and his girlfriend - as he poses in new photoshoot

By LUKE AUGUSTUS, ASSISTANT SPORTS EDITOR

Published: 06:36 EST, 9 November 2026 | Updated: 08:45 EST, 9 November 2026

Declan Rice has opened up about reducing his use of social media - citing the mental toll it can have.

The Arsenal and England midfielder has been subjected to online abuse in the past - with his long-term partner Lauren Fryer having also been targeted.

Online abuse about her appearance had led to Fryer wiping all posts from her Instagram page back in 2024.

While Rice still has an Instagram account, he quit X three years ago as a result of the negativity that app can bring.

'I've not been back on the app since,' he told GQ Magazine - after being named among their Men of the Year. 'You could be having a great morning. You read one thing, and it just ruins your whole day.

'I feel like that's one of my strongest points... the ability to block out noise and not deal with that kind of stuff.'

Declan Rice has revealed he quit X three years ago due to the abuse he would receive online

Rice and long-term partner Lauren Fryer have been targeted in the past (pair pictured with their son Jude in May)

The midfielder says one post could 'ruin' his whole day - even if he was a great day beforehand

Rice, who has a four-year-old son called Jude with Fryer, is worried about the impact of social media on the younger generations.

'Screen time, for me, isn't a big thing,' he says of his own personal phone usage. 'I think social media for kids is 100 per cent a problem. I don't feel like there's as much personality now as there was a few years ago.'

As detailed by Daily Mail Sport last month, the 27-year-old is closing in on a new long-term deal at Arsenal believed to be worth around £350,000 per week.

The contract will put him on a level footing with Bukayo Saka at the top of the club's salary list.

And speaking about money in football as a whole, Rice told GQ Magazine that the finances within the sport are 'ridiculous' and despite his wealth he still wants to set a good example to his son Jude.

'The money that's in football as a whole is just ridiculous,' he continued. 'It's important to understand what things cost, even if you don't have to worry about paying for them.

'You still want to teach your kid values, not wanting them to be spoiled. You want them to work and to understand the value of life and money.

'It makes you more comfortable to be able to do things. But I don't wake up and think, "Oh, I've got loads of money", it's not the end goal.'

The 27-year-old is worried about the pitfalls of social media on the younger generations

The 27-year-old Arsenal and England star has been named in GQ Magazine's Men of the Year

Rice's focus will now be on his country with England hosting Croatia on Thursday night in the UEFA Nations League before travelling to World Cup champions Spain on Sunday.

Going into the November international break, the midfielder is enjoying a fine season with Arsenal who sit six points clear at the top of the Premier League with 27 points from their opening 10 matches.

#2086. Lazio, strategia chiara per gennaio: confermati tutti i big, piace un ex Serie A

Nessuna partenza prevista a gennaio per i titolari biancocelesti: la società si muove in anticipo valutando rinforzi offensivi e giovani talenti.

La Lazio si avvicina alla sessione di mercato  invernale muovendosi con largo anticipo: ad un mese e mezzo dall'apertura del mercato, il club biancoceleste comincia a pianificare le mosse per rinforzare la squadra. Secondo TuttoMercatoWeb, la dirigenza avrebbe già le idee chiare su come muoversi nel mese di gennaio.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

Il direttore sportivo Fabiani ha fatto chiarezza sulle strategie societarie, annunciando che a gennaio non si muoverà nessun top. La dirigenza intende respingere al mittente qualsiasi assalto per i pezzi pregiati della rosa: i vari Taylor, Nuno Tavares, Isaksen e tutti gli altri protagonisti resteranno stabilmente a Roma.

Per quanto concerne il reparto avanzato, il nome di Icardi resta una semplice idea senza riscontri concreti. Discorso differente per gli esterni offensivi, dove piace molto il profilo di Dennis Man, attualmente al PSV Eindhoven ma con un passato nel Parma e un gradimento per un eventuale ritorno in Serie A. Prima di affondare il colpo con un'offerta basata su un prestito con diritto di riscatto, tuttavia, la dirigenza dovrà valutare la situazione legata a Cancellieri. Inoltre, gli occhi del direttore sportivo sono puntati anche sui giovani: piace molto il capitano dell'Under 19 tedesca, il centrocampista classe 2008 Ebu Bekir Is, di proprietà dell'Eintracht Francoforte e già accostato anche allo Sporting Braga.

#2085. Ex-EFL player picks out ‘massive issue’ at West Ham at the moment

West Ham are positioned in 9th place in the Championship table after their first 15 fixtures of the season. WHUFC are one point off the play-offs behind QPR and Southampton.

The Hammers lost 2-1 away at Blackburn Rovers last time out before the international break at Ewood Park. They are back in action next weekend with a home clash against Preston North End at the London Stadium as they aim to return to winning ways.

Parkin, who used to play for the likes of Ipswich Town, Luton Town and Swindon Town, has delivered his honest verdict on the London outfit on the Benjamin Bloom YouTube channel: “They’re conceding far too many goals. They’ve been incredibly open in the last few games. They showed great powers of recovery in that game at Cardiff. Defensively is a massive issue.”

He added: “There’s not much Championship experience. Who are those leaders if you’re putting guys fresh into this XI in a really challenging league where you’re going to be so tested in every game. Every game at the London Stadium is the biggest for the opposition.”

West Ham will get the chance to bolster their options at the back with some reinforcements in the upcoming January transfer window. WHUFC will have the opportunity to freshen up their ranks ahead of the second-half of the campaign.

The Hammers have conceded 23 goals already this term. They have the worst defensive record in the top 10 right now.

At the other end of the pitch, they have no problems scoring goals and have found the net on a whopping 37 occasions. In fact, they have the best attack in the division.

West Ham have turned to former midfielder Scott Parker as their new head coach. He was picked as their replacement for Nuno Espirito Santo, who was given the sack after a poor run of form.

West Ham would benefit from a new goalkeeper. WHUFC’s number one Mads Hermansen has struggled over recent times, whilst Alphonse Areola has slipped out of favour.

The Hammers could also do with another centre-back to help them to stop leaking goals. They need someone to compete with Konstantinos Mavropanos and Max Kilman.

Parker is an experienced coach and knows what it takes to get out of the second tier. He has guided Fulham, AFC Bournemouth and Burnley up in the past.

It may take him a little time to get used to his new squad and patience will have to be required from the fanbase. When West Ham, they should have enough quality to rise back up the league and challenge for the top two.

They will fancy their chances of a win against Preston North End next Saturday. Their next opponents are in the drop zone with Burnley and Derby County.

#2083. Wayne Rooney and Paddy McGuinness laud football super-agent Paul Stretford - who has worked with Man United icon since his teens - at star-studded Global Football Industry Awards

Published: 13:14 EST, 9 November 2026 | Updated: 13:15 EST, 9 November 2026

Wayne Rooney has led the praise for football super-agent Paul Stretford as the industry heavyweight was honoured at the Global Football Industry Awards.

Stretford, alongside Kenneth Shepherd, received the prestigious Lifetime Achievement Award at this year's Awards in recognition of their longstanding careers and work on Soccer Aid for Unicef.

He has worked by Rooney's side for more than 20 years, and has recently appeared on the former footballer's reality TV show, which he fronts alongside wife Coleen.

Rooney presented Stretford with the award himself, alongside Toni Duggan and comedian Paddy McGuinness. Theo Walcott and Jamie Carragher hosted the ceremony.

Rooney said of his agent and close friend: 'Paul in particular has had a major influence on my life since I was a teenager and has guided my career.

'Both Paul and Kenneth are very deserving of this award. It is not before time and I'm delighted for both of them. The work they've done on Soccer Aid is phenomenal too.'

Wayne Rooney (centre) has lauded close friend and football super agent Paul Stretford (right)

Stretford doesn't just look after Rooney, having also worked with the likes of Harry Maguire since founding Proactive Sports Management, now known as Triple S Sports Group, in 1987.

Shepherd, meanwhile, has been instrumental in the success of the Soccer Aid initiative which has raised more than £137million for Unicef since it launched in 2006.

McGuinness added: 'I'm so proud to pay tribute to a duo who have had a profound impact on football and two figures we've both had the pleasure of working with over the years. Together, they built the Triple S Group into a dynamic force in British and international football.'

England assistant manager Anthony Barry also carried out an exclusive Q&A at the event, which saw Tyler Alexander-Arnold, brother of England defender Trent, also scoop a top award.

The brother of the Real Madrid star represents Jordan Henderson, Jarrod Bowen and Andy Robertson as well as his sibling, who saw his PLG Agency bought by Gersh talent agency in May of this year.

#2082. Birmingham City’s Top 3 players of the 2026/27 season so far, including star Premier League loanee

Birmingham City currently sit top of the Championship with the November international break upon us. After a tough start, BCFC have been on fire in recent weeks with their latest result being a vital 2-1 win over high-flying Swansea City.

Chris Davies has managed to silence a lot of the doubters as there were some fans who were pessimistic going into this season. So far, the Blues have had a way better start to the campaign than what was expected in the summer.

It has been a real team effort to rise to such a strong position, but the break offers a good chance to highlight some individual stars. In no particular order, we put forward Birmingham City’s top three players of the 2026/27 season so far…

Liam Millar has previously had strong seasons in the Championship with Preston North End and Hull City. Despite this, It is safe to say this season, the Canadian has taken a major step up since joining the Blues.

Playing on the left wing, Millar has been one of Birmingham City’s best attacking outlets this season. The 27-year-old already has seven goal contributions in 11 appearances in the Championship this term.

Birmingham City fans were originally very disappointed when they failed to re-sign Ibrahim Osman in the summer. However, it is clear that Davies highly rates Millar and the winger has been a threat in every game he has played this season.

Even in matches where Millar doesn’t grab a goal contribution, he is heavily involved in BCFC’s success. Birmingham City’s most recent match against Swansea City shows this, as Millar created one big chance, had three successful dribbles and two shots on target (FotMob).

German centre-back Phil Neumann has had a big turnaround in form this season. In his debut campaign, he struggled to consistently keep his spot in the Blues defence, but he is now irreplaceable in Davies’ lineup.

After the signing of Dael Fry, it looked like Neumann would have his place taken by the new arrival given his strong performances with Middlesbrough. However, the 29-year-old has been arguably the best centre-back in the league so far this season.

His speed and ability on the ball have been vital part of Birmingham City’s strong start to the season. His defensive contributions have been particularly impressive with 115 clearances, 25 tackles and 26 interceptions (FotMob).

There will be no surprise that the two centre-backs have featured in this top three. Klarer and Neumann have been arguably the main reason that Birmingham City have conceded the least amount of goals in the Championship this season, with 14.

The Austrian is the backbone of Davies’ side as he is the captain and has been absolutely colossal at the back. His ability to win duels and clear any danger is remarkable. According to FotMob, he is amongst the best centre-backs in the league when it comes to aerial duels won with 105 and recoveries with 60.

Klarer was already extremely highly rated after his performances in 2025/26, but he has been near faultless for BCFC this term.

#2079. 3 players who West Ham should offload in January, including £27m striker

A poor run of form has left West Ham outside the Championship play-offs in 9th, with several high-earning bench-warmers potentially looking to seek pastures new. WHUFC will also be keen to bolster their squad with new stars, but equally, shifting those on hefty wages could prove to be just as important.

New boss Scott Parker has taken charge of two games so far, salvaging a point at Cardiff City before losing to Blackburn Rovers. He will undoubtedly use this international break to assess which players are part of his plans for the remainder of the season and beyond.

Here, we take a look at three players West Ham should look to offload in the upcoming window as they bid to streamline their squad.

Despite the poor form of Mads Hermansen in the early stages of the campaign, the Danish shot-stopper is the undisputed number one with Alphonse Areola.

Areola hasn’t been the go-to number one since the 2023/24 campaign and is yet to feature this term. Having played a key role in their UEFA Europa Conference League triumph, the 33-year-old was one of several players West Ham tried to part company with in the summer, but his wages proved a stumbling block.

Academy graduate Finlay Herrick has shown plenty of promise and appears ahead of Areola in the pecking order, so a January departure may suit all parties to facilitate other moves.

Another example of a player who was keen to depart in the summer, Soungoutou Magassa has played just 45 minutes of football this season after strong links with Crystal Palace.

The move to West Ham’s London neighbours failed to come to fruition, and he was far down the pecking order under Nuno Espirito Santo. Magassa started at Cardiff City but failed to make an impression and was benched again versus Blackburn Rovers, with the likes of Arne Engels and Tomas Soucek more suited to Parker’s system in a deeper midfield role.

A January departure from WHUFC looks inevitable at the right price, and would likely suit all parties despite the belief in his potential.

After signing for £27m from Lazio in January, Taty Castellanos has notched a solitary Championship goal this season.

Rumours of a summer departure were put to bed and he was viewed as a key figure in helping to spearhead West Ham’s promotion push. However, the Argentinian striker has often been criticised for his work rate and has struggled to replicate the form he showed last term.

River Plate reportedly view the 28-year-old as a priority target in their striker search, although the finances involved in the deal could prove to be a major hurdle. However, cashing in could give WHUFC the chance to sign a true talisman to aid their promotion bid.

#2077. A failed spell at Wolves almost saw him walk away from football ...now Danny Armstrong is idolised in Romania as he attempts to lead Dinamo Bucharest back to former glories

Published: 13:42 EST, 9 November 2026 | Updated: 13:52 EST, 9 November 2026

The daughter says: ‘Da’. Darby Armstrong, all of two years old, is not calling out for her father, Danny.

‘She is saying “Yes” in Romanian. She can count to ten in Romanian,’ says her proud father. She may have to learn to count higher, and quickly, to chart her father’s figures in the Superliga with Dinamo Bucharest.

Armstrong sits in an upmarket restaurant in the beautiful suburbs of Bucharest with a park stretching towards a lake. He sips ginger tea with honey and engages with waiters, diners and children who approach him for a chat and a photograph.

‘Darby is a bit bemused by it,’ he says. ‘We were in the mall there and a guy wanted a pic. I could see her looking up to see what all the fuss was about.’

Armstrong could be forgiven for being ever so slightly bemused himself. At 29, he has taken a few dunts and not just from enthusiastic full-backs. Success has hardly been overnight.

‘I nearly gave up once,’ he says quietly. ‘Another time I thought it was all over and I was looking at one-to-one coaching or being a personal trainer.’

Danny Armstrong is hailed after his match-winning heroics in a crucial win over UTA Arad

He survived, though. He is a hero at Dinamo. There is a clamour on matchday when he comes out of the tunnel. The ultras, high behind a goal, chant his name with a fervour that suddenly breaks out in red flares and manic jumping.

It is a long way from Ayr. Armstrong knows he did not walk it alone. His saviours include John McGlynn, who brought him to Raith Rovers, George Cairns, who convinced him to stay at Hamilton, and Scott Sellars, the youth coach who advised him to leave Wolverhampton Wanderers for first team football.

He also took inspiration from many team-mates, including Kieran Tierney who was once his room-mate in a Scotland under-age team,

‘I was meant to be rooming with another left-back but he pulled out and this young player from Celtic came in,’ recalls Armstrong. ‘He wasn’t even in the squad initially and now he is Scotland’s best left-back alongside Robbo.

‘I used to play against KT so many times and I have great respect for him, playing so many games for Celtic, getting that move to Arsenal and coming back to Scotland to win trophies. The setbacks he has had… he’s an inspiration.

‘He has resilience. He has motivation. He took the half opportunity that was offered to him and ran with it.’

Armstrong has done the same in Bucharest. He has been bolstered by his past. ‘It builds you,’ he says of his experience. ‘There have been tough spells but you can look back and make sense of them and see how they have helped you. Football takes a lot of work.’

Armstrong has thrived in Romania since making the leftfield switch to Dinamo Bucharest

It takes a lot of belief too. That can be a capricious quality, seemingly dependent on the winds of fortune.

Armstrong can point to two hat-tricks that gave him faith in a time of need. ‘I scored a hat-trick for Hamilton in a youth game at Auchenhowie against Rangers,’ he says. ‘That came after I had been convinced to stick at it.’

He also scored three last season in the derby against Steaua. ‘That was crazy,’ he says. ‘I don’t think I have realised even yet how big it was. People tell me it was the greatest day in their lives. We hadn’t beaten them in five or six years. We were also in a bad spot with poor form. And then I score three. It gives me goosebumps even now.’

He adds: ‘There is a real football culture here. It means so much to people and I love that. It actually wasn’t too hard to come here because I had looked at it a year earlier but stayed on at Kilmarnock. When Dinamo came in and showed me videos and talked about the history, I was on board.’

There was one decisive factor. ‘I knew it was time to try to make a name for myself somewhere else. I wanted to test myself, keep myself open to other opportunities, try to get to the highest level I can get.’

He is professionally coy about the prospect of international football, having again missed out on Sebastien Pocognoli’s latest Scotland squad. He maintains the line of concentrating on his club form, having a dream to play for his country, and hoping to be noticed.

Armstrong was a big hit at Kilmarnock and has long been mooted for a Scotland call-up

His performances in Romania, though, have been so outstanding that there may be the odd glance from the Pocognoli camp at just what is happening at Dinamo. They may have to be quick as Armstrong qualifies for Ireland too.

He plays in a wide right role, with the freedom to cut inside and become a playmaker. His manager, Nuno Campos, has faith in him. He is the latest in a line of characters who have led him from doubt to conviction.

There is no hesitation. ‘It was McGlynn who saved me,’ says Armstrong. But there were previous tribulations that required the intervention of football folk and Armstrong is immensely grateful.

‘I had been going up to Hamilton from Ayr every night as a kid, playing at the weekend, and by the time I was about 15 I think I was drained. I felt I was missing out on other things in life. It was tough on my parents, too, who did everything for me. The family always sacrificed themselves for me.’

His parents told George Cairns, Hamilton coach, that their son did not want to continue. The reply was instant: ‘Get him up here and I will have a chat with him.’

The crisis passed after this talk and that hat-trick against Rangers. But further tribulations were to come.

Armstrong's relentless drive and enthusiasm have made him a standout player in Romania

Armstrong headed to Wolverhampton Wanderers, spurning an offer by Rangers under Mark Warburton and Davie Weir. ‘That was a mistake,’ he says. He was never near first team football at Wolves though the club wanted to retain him. After a chat with Scott Sellars, he took the chance to leave and start again.

‘That was very, very difficult,’ he says. ‘I did not play from May to November. I was sitting by the phone, urging it to ring with an offer. I had thoughts about packing it all in and going into coaching or being a personal trainer.’

Then an unlikely Superman came to the rescue in the shape of McGlynn, then Raith manager. ‘That changed everything, everything,’ says Armstrong.

A bounce game was organised to assess the player but Armstrong admits the team’s performance was awful. ‘I was very much part of that,’ he says.

He did much better in another friendly but budget considerations meant McGlynn could offer only a nine-game contract. ‘It was a couple of hundred quid a week but it meant everything to me.’ Five goals in nine games led to an extended contract.

‘He is a genius,’ says Armstrong. ‘He is so big on detail, detail, detail. He is a fantastic football coach. It’s an obsession for him.

‘I have never met a man like him. He saved my football career and made me believe I could make it.’

John McGlynn helped save Armstrong's career by taking him under his wing at Raith Rovers

After a spell at Ross County, Armstrong flourished under Derek McInnes at Kilmarnock and the move to Romania offered further scope for growth.

‘It was a bit difficult at first settling in but now my partner, Lauren, and Darby love it. We have made friends,’ he says.

At this a couple of waiters approach the table with grins and cheery banter. Armstrong responds with a warm handshake and that half-embrace that signals another acquiescence to yet another picture.

‘The big difference this season is the coach,’ says Armstrong. ‘He has come in and completely changed the environment and the atmosphere. He has brought fun back and he makes you want to come into training.’

Dinamo are now sitting joint-top of the league. ‘The first goal has to be European football, getting this club back to where it belongs,’ he says.

Armstrong has his sights set on an historic Romanian title and is not ruling out a longer stay

The Dinamo fans, who have not witnessed a league title in 20 years, sense Superliga glory. ‘We are a good side and we play nice stuff,’ says Armstrong. ‘But let’s take it game by game.’

He has the same laid-back attitude to his contract. ‘It expires at the end of the season and there is an option there but we leave all that to after Christmas.’

He is happy in Bucharest but knows the fate of a footballer is never predictable. ‘It is a wonderful place. The fans are great and the manager has faith in me.’

He points out that Campos wants him to be a ‘monster’. This is an instruction to be powerful and innovative in the final third.

The next night Armstrong swivels just outside the box and hits a wonderful shot to open the scoring against UTA Arad.

He then sprints towards the stand where Lauren and Darby are celebrating. He has just been a monster. But he is still a dad, in whatever language.

#2074. Southampton must join race for West Ham, Wolves target as ideal Cyle Larin competition: Opinion

Southampton’s reliance on Cyle Larin has become increasingly apparent this season, making the January transfer window an important opportunity to strengthen their attacking options. After Ross Stewart’s release, Divin Mubama joined on loan, but he has not made the desired impact.

Rangers striker Ryan Naderi is a man who may be available in January could be an ideal addition for the Saints. Offering the physical presence, work rate and goalscoring instincts that Tonda Eckert’s side need, they would need to beat West Ham and Wolves to his signature.

Larin has been outstanding since making his move permanent in the summer, following his productive loan spell from Mallorca. The Canadian international quickly established himself as Southampton’s main attacking threat, scoring seven goals in the early stages of the Championship campaign and earning the division’s Player of the Month award for August.

However, his recent hamstring injury has exposed a concerning lack of reliable alternatives. Lewis Dobbin has struggled for consistency since arriving for around £9m, while Mubama has yet to establish himself. With Southampton targeting promotion, relying so heavily on one striker is a considerable risk.

Naderi could help address that problem. The 23-year-old has emerged as one of Rangers’ most important players, registering nine goals and five assists in 17 appearances across all competitions this season.

His Scottish Premiership return of six goals and three assists in 11 matches underlines his growing influence. There are obvious similarities between Naderi and Larin. Both are powerful, penalty-box forwards who use their movement, physicality and finishing instincts to make an impact.

At 6ft 4in, Naderi would offer even greater aerial presence, while his relentless pressing and willingness to run beyond defenders could suit Eckert’s energetic approach. Crucially, he would provide genuine competition rather than simply another attacking option.

Larin remains Southampton’s first-choice striker, but the 31-year-old cannot be expected to shoulder the goalscoring responsibility throughout a demanding 46-game campaign. Naderi could rotate with him, provide a different physical threat or potentially complement him in a two-striker system.

There are complications, however. Rangers reportedly have little interest in selling midway through the season, particularly with Naderi under contract until 2029.

Interest from West Ham and Wolves could also increase the financial competition, meaning Southampton would likely need to make a significant investment. Nevertheless, this is precisely the type of recruitment they should be considering. Naderi is young, improving and already producing consistently at a major club.

Southampton need greater depth around Larin, and signing a striker with a similar physical and goalscoring profile could make a significant difference to their promotion ambitions.

#2073. Liam Scales has no doubt 'amazing' Kasper Hogh will soon bring an end to his worrying Celtic goal drought

By JOHN MCGARRY, SCOTTISH CHIEF SPORTS FEATURE WRITER

Published: 14:09 EST, 9 November 2026 | Updated: 14:54 EST, 9 November 2026

Liam Scales claims he’s got no fears about Kasper Hogh’s luck turning after the Dane’s goalless run at Celtic extended to 16 matches.

Without a goal to his name since hitting a hat-trick at Kilmarnock on August 9, the forward thought his drought was over when he found the net against the same opponent on Sunday.

Denied his long-awaited moment of glory when VAR spotted an earlier infringement by Mika Baur, Hogh watched from the bench as Martin O’Neill’s side ground out a one-goal win which took them four points clear at the top of the table.

But while the Dane will now have to wait until the home match with St Mirren following the international break for his next opportunity, Scales – the scorer on the day – feels his team-mate has nothing to worry about.

‘He’s been brilliant,’ said the Irishman. ‘He keeps doing the right things and eventually it will fall for him.

‘He was so unlucky with his goal being ruled out. And he told me he was right behind me with the goal I scored. If I’d known that I might have left it!

Liam Scales was Celtic's weekend goal hero but he is tipping Kasper Hogh to come good

‘It will fall for him. He’s a goalscorer and a good player. His all-round game was brilliant, he held the ball up really well.

‘He’s just been unlucky. But his attitude has been brilliant. If he keeps doing the right things it will work out for him.

‘He has an amazing attitude and that’s why I really believe he will be successful.’

With Benjamin Nygren missing a second-half penalty, O’Neill was grateful for the improvised finish of Scales earlier in the game.

‘It took an age to come down,’ Scales recalled of his third of the season. ‘I wasn’t sure if it was going to land on the roof of the net.

‘I was just trying to guide it on target and luckily enough it went in. It was nice to score and hopefully I can get a few more between now and the end of the season.’

The victory over Killie ensured Celtic bounced back from their loss at Falkirk and a draw with Dundee United and built on their dramatic midweek win over Besiktas in the Europa League.

‘It was definitely important,’ said Scales. ‘Two games without a win is bad enough, but three would have been a nightmare.

Hogh thought he had ended his scoring drought at Rugby Park but his effort was ruled out

‘We knew we needed that win and can go into the break and refresh. Some of the lads can rest and recover and hopefully we’ll come back in this form.

‘I do think confidence is a massive part of our group and it was probably hit a little bit. The win against a top side like Besiktas has boosted us a little bit. There’s kind of a better mood around and we just want to keep adding good results.’

Scales is optimistic that the better options that are now at the manager’s disposal can help the side hit the kind of form which has eluded them this season.

‘We had (Hyun-jun) Yang back on Sunday, Jota is obviously starting,’ he said. ‘These guys are getting back to full sharpness and getting minutes. Hopefully, this will help and we can build on it.

‘We know we haven’t been good enough between the last international window and this one. We’ve let a few results go and we’re not exactly where we want to be.

‘We always want to be on top of the table. We don’t want to be chasing. We want to be good arm’s length from the challengers.

‘Last season wasn’t what we wanted at all and we don’t want a repeat of that.’

#2072. Dal Milan... all'università, Origi si è iscritto ad Harvard: "Voglio perfezionare il mio lato business"

Divock Origi ha deciso di tornare a studiare. L'ex attaccante del Milan, che ha ufficializzato il proprio ritiro dal calcio giocato l'8 giugno 2026 all'età di soli 31 anni, ha scelto di riprendere in mano i libri di scuola e iscriversi a diversi percorsi formativi, tra cui quello della prestigiosa Harvard Business School. Intervistato dalla testata belga HBVL, ha raccontato le ragioni della sua scelta: "Finora tutti hanno visto solo il mio lato calcistico. Ma dentro di me si celano diversi aspetti. Attualmente sono tornato a studiare, fino ad arrivare ad Harvard. Voglio perfezionare il mio lato imprenditoriale e combinarlo eventualmente con la mia passione per la moda e il design". Il programma Crossover Into Business, della durata di un semestre, è pensato per accompagnare proprio gli atleti professionisti nel passaggio al mondo degli affari.

L'ex attaccante belga non si limita però alla formazione imprenditoriale. Frequenta anche corsi in alcune delle più importanti scuole di moda, come ESMOD in Francia e Central Saint Martins a Londra, collaborando con professionisti del settore. "Lavoro insieme a professionisti del massimo livello, quindi in questo modo puoi fare progressi molto più rapidamente in una passione che sto coltivando. Prima voglio capire come funziona il mondo della moda. Poi guarderò oltre", ha spiegato. Origi ha inoltre visitato la Fashion Week di Parigi e Tokyo per approfondire il rapporto tra moda e cultura.

Il calcio, tuttavia, non è completamente uscito dalla sua vita. L'ex calciatore del Liverpool è infatti coinvolto nell'agenzia DLF Sports, fondata insieme all'ex compagno Willem Ofori per accompagnare giovani talenti nel loro percorso professionale. Ciò che non ha invece intenzione di intraprendere è la carriera da allenatore: "Ho molta stima dei tecnici, ma non mi vedo assolutamente in quel ruolo", ha concluso. Dopo i trionfi coi Reds, tra Premier e Champions, e la parentesi sfortunata al Milan, per Origi è cominciata dunque una nuova partita, questa volta lontano dal rettangolo verde.

#2070. Birmingham City have a number nine problem to solve when August Priske returns from injury: Opinion

Birmingham City have plenty of reasons to be optimistic about their Championship promotion push, but Jay Stansfield’s recent performances could prove particularly significant. The 23-year-old scored twice against Swansea City, including an 87th-minute winner, to send BCFC top of the table.

He also passed 50 goals for the club before his 24th birthday, underlining his importance since arriving as their record signing. However, it is not just his finishing that makes Stansfield so valuable.

His recent return to the central striker position suggests Chris Davies may have found the best way to get the most out of his €18m-rated forward. However, it may cause an issue with top-scorer August Priske returning from injury.

Across 15 starts and 1,275 Championship minutes this season, Stansfield has registered four goals and one assist, averaging a 7.26 FotMob rating. His 42 shots, 20 efforts on target and 4.88 expected goals demonstrate a consistent appetite for getting into dangerous positions.

Stansfield has frequently operated in attacking midfield during his Birmingham career, but his historical numbers make a compelling argument for keeping him further forward. In 71 appearances as a striker, he has scored 29 goals. By comparison, 46 outings as an attacking midfielder have produced just 12 goals.

That difference is particularly relevant with Priske recently absent. Stansfield has stepped into the central role and responded with three goals across the Millwall and Swansea fixtures, despite accumulating just 1.09 xG in those matches. Against Swansea alone, he registered seven shots and earned a 9.1 FotMob rating.

Crucially, playing centrally does not mean sacrificing his work rate. Stansfield has recorded 39 defensive contributions, including 22 tackles, eight interceptions and 65 recoveries this season.

He is not a conventional target man, winning just 28.6% of his aerial duels, but his mobility, pressing and ability to link play make him a different kind of centre-forward. FotMob’s positional data also highlights why moving him deeper is not necessarily beneficial.

His shot volume and defensive contributions are strengths, whereas his relatively low touches suggest he is not someone who needs to dominate possession to influence matches.

Birmingham invested heavily in Stansfield because of his goalscoring ability, and his record already justifies that faith. With a contract until 2031, there is little reason to reinvent his role.

Priske’s eventual return could give Davies another attacking option, but Stansfield should remain the first-choice striker. The recent evidence suggests Birmingham have a better chance of maximising their record signing when he is leading the line rather than supporting someone else.

#2068. Wrexham should revisit Pavel Sulc situation after latest Lyon revelation: Opinion

Wrexham have had a very poor start to the season considering their finish to last year. However, they are a couple of strong January additions away from pushing themselves up towards the Championship play-off picture. One target could be a returning bid for Lyon’s Pavel Sulc.

Phil Parkinson’s side reportedly made the highest offer for the Lyon attacker in the summer of 2026, with their bid understood to be worth approximately £35m. However, Sulc rejected the opportunity, admitting he was not attracted by the prospect of playing in the Championship or moving to a small town in Wales.

That rejection appeared to end Wrexham’s hopes, but Lyon’s financial difficulties have created a potential second opportunity. The French club must sell players during the January window to satisfy financial commitments, and Sulc has emerged as one of their leading candidates for departure.

The 25-year-old has established himself as an exciting attacking talent since joining Lyon from Viktoria Plzen for an initial €7.5m in August 2025. Under contract until June 2029, he can operate as an attacking midfielder, striker or left winger, offering considerable versatility.

His underlying numbers also demonstrate why Wrexham were prepared to spend so heavily. He has 11 goals and three assists in 27 Ligue 1 appearances during his first season at Lyon.

He has also made an encouraging start to 2026/27, registering one goal and three assists in five league appearances, alongside another goal in the Europa League.

His creativity is particularly appealing. Sulc recorded eight chances created in just 417 Ligue 1 minutes this season, while his ability to contribute goals from several attacking positions would address one of Wrexham’s biggest weaknesses.

Parkinson’s side have struggled for attacking consistency, with injuries to Sam Smith, Josh Windass, Nathan Broadhead and Callum O’Hare leaving them short of creativity and firepower. Sitting around 15th in the Championship, they need reinforcements if their promotion ambitions are to remain realistic.

There are, however, two significant complications. Sulc underwent surgery on a meniscus injury in October, with his return potentially falling anywhere between January and March. Lyon’s reduced negotiating position could also make a deal more affordable, although his injury represents a genuine availability risk.

His previous comments about the Championship and Wrexham’s location cannot be ignored either. Nevertheless, the club’s continued investment and ambitions could offer a different proposition several months later.

Wrexham should not assume Sulc has changed his mind, but the sporting circumstances and Lyon’s financial position have shifted. Given their need for attacking quality, reopening the conversation would certainly be worthwhile.

#2062. Falkirk boss John McGlynn deserves big move, says Craig Levein, as Aberdeen sack Stephen Robinson

By JOHN MCGARRY, SCOTTISH CHIEF SPORTS FEATURE WRITER

Published: 17:30 EST, 9 November 2026 | Updated: 17:30 EST, 9 November 2026

Craig Levein believes John McGlynn ‘ticks every box’ to manage a bigger club than Falkirk — as Aberdeen begin the search for Stephen Robinson’s successor.

The Northern Irishman was sacked last night after eight months in charge, with the Dons’ 4-0 home loss to Hibs on Saturday proving to be the last straw.

With the Pittodrie side now sitting second bottom of the Premiership, chairman Dave Cormack, sporting director Lutz Pfannenstiel and newly-appointed chief executive Adrian Bevington will soon begin the process of trying to identify the man they believe can take the team forward.

And Levein, a former Hearts colleague of McGlynn’s, believes the veteran’s record of taking the Bairns from League One to third place in the top flight should speak for itself.

'John has passed every test that’s been put in front of him and I don’t know what he needs to do,’ said Levein.

‘Falkirk supporters, of course, won’t like me saying that, but I’ve worked with the guy. He’s got integrity, knowledge, he delves into the minutiae when he’s doing his team talks and makes sure everybody knows what’s expected.

Falkirk boss John McGlynn has guided them from League One to third in the Premiership

Aberdeen boss Stephen Robinson was sacked after eight months in charge at Pittodrie

‘I’m biased, I like the guy, but I think he’s done a wonderful job. He’s done all the work that he needs to do in my eyes to get an opportunity.’

McGlynn worked at Hearts as a coach under Jim Jefferies and went on to manage the Gorgie club twice — once as an interim.

Prior to taking charge of Falkirk in 2022, he had two spells at Raith Rovers, a stint at Livingston and a scouting role at Celtic under Brendan Rodgers.

Despite completely turning the Bairns’ fortunes around, Levein believes that there remains a strange reluctance in some quarters to recognise the 64-year-old as one of the shrewdest managers in the country.

‘I don’t know what it is, looking at what he’s done, his whole body of work,’ shrugged Levein.

‘When I went to Hearts at the tail end of my career, I was helping John do some training with the youngsters. Even then, you could tell immediately that he knows what he’s talking about.

‘He spent a fair bit of time with Brendan at Celtic. After that, I think he adopted Brendan’s style of football with some changes and altered two or three bits.

‘Essentially, he went to Celtic, watched and learned everything on top of the knowledge he already had. His team at Raith started to play in the same way as Celtic did. Then he’s continued that when he’s gone to Falkirk.

‘I was promoting him for the Scotland job when it came around. If you look at his record, it’s pretty impressive.

‘I just think he is undervalued, maybe not by the supporters that have had him as their manager, but certainly in a wider sense in Scotland. He has done a brilliant job so far.

‘I’ll come back to the fact I’m biased. I like the guy. I like what he does at football clubs. If I’m a chairman, all day long I’d be looking for somebody like him.

‘He’s meticulous in his work and everything’s done properly. He stays within the bandwidth of emotions.

‘He doesn’t get overly emotional during matches and he stays in his area, which I think is good for him and good for his players.

‘You don’t often see him throwing stuff about in the dugout or anything like that. He’s a top manager who has improved enormously in the last five or six years.’

Craig Levein believes McGlynn has done more than enough to merit a move to a bigger club

Robinson’s departure was confirmed yesterday in a statement.

Despite winning the League Cup with St Mirren a year ago and guiding the Buddies to three straight top-six finishes, he never enjoyed a consistent run of results at Pittodrie.

‘Following a board meeting, Aberdeen Football Club has today taken the decision to part company with head coach Stephen Robinson,’ the statement read.

‘Stephen leaves the club alongside assistant head coach Brian Kerr and first team coach Stephen Craigan. We thank them for their efforts during their time at Pittodrie and wish them every success for the future.

‘Preparatory work to support the appointment of a new manager has been undertaken, and the recruitment process is ongoing. The focus will be on identifying the right candidate to lead the club forward and deliver the progress expected across all areas of the football operation.

‘Stuart Duff will assume interim charge of first team training while the club continues its search for a new head coach.’

#2061. Data estimates how much Huddersfield Town loan star Cameron Humphreys is worth ahead of potential permanent swoop

Huddersfield Town are the team to watch in League One this season. HTAFC sit 1st in the table and, despite a first-round exit of the FA Cup, will be hopeful of a return to the Championship.

The Terries have seen several outstanding performers with the likes of Derensili Sanches Fernandes and Joe Taylor. However, it’s Cameron Humphreys who has come to life in the 2026/27 campaign under Martin Drury.

Huddersfield Town signed the 23-year-old on loan for the second consecutive term in the summer, and he has established himself already with five assists in 13 games. If he continues at the same level, the Terriers may have to consider turning his spell into a permanent deal.

Humphreys is under contract at Ipswich Town until 2028, so if they cash in on the midfielder, they could demand a relatively substantial fee. However, given the fact he rose through the academy at Portman Road and would generate absolute profit, he could go for a cheaper fee.

That is what the data suggests, and Huddersfield Town would certainly entertain a move for the midfielder if his value is accurate. FootballTransfers value the youngster at €700,000, which is just under £600,000.

Given his displays in League One this season and the current market rate for most 23-year-olds in the ranks of a Premier League side, it wouldn’t be a surprise to see ITFC actually demand a seven-figure sum to acquire Humphreys.

The midfielder has won 57% of his duels this season and has won 32 fouls for HTAFC. With eight big chances created and 18 successful crosses, Ipswich Town will undoubtedly view his price tag as a lot higher should bids come in for him in 2027.

Huddersfield Town’s main chance of signing Humphreys comes down to whether or not Ipswich Town remain in the Premier League by the end of the campaign. The 23-year-old is excelling in League One this season, and if they were in the Championship again, the Tractor Boys would likely include him in their plans.

That being said, HTAFC currently sit top of the third tier, and promotion to the second tier would give them a great pull to sign the midfielder permanently. Drury has the Terriers playing a good brand of football, and if Humphreys were a part of his plans, there’s no reason why he wouldn’t want to join.

The 23-year-old has made just 44 appearances for Ipswich Town since his debut in 2021. However, since the 2023/24 campaign, he has appeared only 14 times for the Tractor Boys and his patience could be running out for a future at Portman Road.

#2060. Stephen Robinson was just the latest Aberdeen manager to crash and burn... it's time to look at the club and its structure

By JOHN MCGARRY, SCOTTISH CHIEF SPORTS FEATURE WRITER

Published: 17:30 EST, 9 November 2026 | Updated: 17:30 EST, 9 November 2026

Across the space of two days last week, Aberdeen fans were dragged down memory lane only to emerge bruised and battered for their troubles.

Friday marked the 40th anniversary of plain old Alex Ferguson’s departure to Manchester United. It was an occasion for newspapers and TV bulletins to again recall the mind-blowing feats which led to the biggest club in Britain beating a path to Pittodrie.

Three Scottish championships, four Scottish Cups, a League Cup, the Cup Winners’ Cup and the Super Cup. The redrawing of the landscape of Scottish football.

There was something especially brutal about how those glories were juxtaposed beside the struggles of the soon-to-be former Dons boss, Stephen Robinson, 24 hours later.

After eight months of grim toil in the Granite City, the four-goal home thrashing the Northern Irishman suffered at the hands of Hibernian on Saturday blew away any lingering hope he retained of turning an increasingly desperate situation around.

A proven manager who was always simply the wrong fit for Aberdeen, Robinson will lick his wounds and come again.

Stephen Robinson is a proven manager who was always the wrong fit  for Aberdeen

But while a failure to live up to Fergie’s impossibly high standards might have done for those in Robinson’s shoes in the years which immediately followed the great man’s move to Old Trafford, no Aberdeen supporter in their right mind views things through that prism these days.

The majority of those who watched Saturday’s capitulation weren’t even born when Fergie’s Reds were running rampage throughout Europe.

They appreciate that the slayings of Real Madrid and Bayern Munich which they watched on YouTube were the highlights of a golden era for the club. One they know is not coming back.

In 2026, the expectation is more modest and hardly unreasonable. That with the fifth-highest average crowd in the country, Aberdeen should be in the top six almost every year, frequently playing in Europe most seasons and winning the odd cup. Yet, they’re miles off that right now.

While Jimmy Thelin did deliver a day in the sun by beating Celtic in the Scottish Cup final last year, that success was an anomaly. There have been just two top-six finishes in the past five years, with two ninth-placed finishes and one in tenth spot.

Given all that, it’s difficult not to recall the old wisdom about being careful what you wished for.

In Derek McInnes’ eight seasons at the club between 2013 and 2021, the team never finished below fourth and were runners-up four years on the spin. The League Cup was also secured in 2014.

A run of consistency and success which supporters had long craved was soon forgotten. McInnes’ familiarity and loyalty were eventually held against him.

Five managers later, Aberdeen appear to be as far away from reclaiming their status as one of the Scottish game’s powerhouses as they’ve ever been.

‘I think we’ve been waiting on it for four or five years,’ said Gothenburg legend Alex McLeish.

‘There’s been some signs, obviously winning the cup that year. You think that’s a wee springboard for the coming season and then it’s all kind of middle of the league, lower end of the league.’

As Robinson cleared his desk last night, even those fans who didn’t want him at the club in the first place would have been reluctant to hold him entirely responsible for the current ills.

The Northern Irishman is demonstrably a good manager. He guided St Mirren to three top-six finishes and the League Cup.

Thelin performed wonders across a long period at Elfsborg. Two years ago, he had Aberdeen sitting on top of the table.

Jim Goodwin was humiliated at Darvel, but now has buoyant Dundee United believing again.

Even Stephen Glass, a left-field choice to succeed McInnes, went on to enjoy success with Memphis 901 and as assistant to Robbie Keane at Ferencvaros.

When a club appoints one manager who doesn’t work out, it’s logical to question the individual. If five managers crash and burn, it says more about the environment and the structure they work in.

‘What is the solution?’ asked McLeish. ‘It’s definitely always down to what kind of players you’ve got.

Jimmy Thelin lifted the Scottish Cup before his Aberdeen career ended in disappointment

‘It’s trying to get a team to function together. To have forwards that you know can score, to have midfielders that can show a bit of quality, beat a man, playing one-twos, being accurate with your passing. Every bit of quality comes into it.

‘The manager will always get the worse side of it, you know, because they can’t sack 20 players.’

While it’s too late to save Robinson, the club do appear to have moved to address this particular shortcoming.

Back in March, Darren Mowbray returned to Pittodrie as head of recruitment three years on from his first stint which saw the captures of Bojan Miovski and Ylber Ramadani.

The trouble is that the mish-mash of players who were recruited by Jordan Miles, Chris Badlan and Nuno de Almeida are plainly not good enough.

Mowbray, it must be said, didn’t get too much right in the summer, either.

It’s not just the failure to sign players with the requisite pedigree and character which dismays the Aberdeen supporters.

When it was clear that Thelin’s time was up in January, the Swede was shown the door without there being a replacement waiting in the wings.

While there were legitimate reasons why Eirik Horneland couldn’t commit at that moment, the Dons’ hierarchy needed a Plan B.

Sandro Schwarz came to the Granite City for talks but nothing materialised, with Peter Leven remaining in interim charge.

With the pressure growing, Robinson – a wholly unpopular choice – was unveiled nine weeks and four days on. None of this reflected well on anyone involved.

Lutz Pfannenstiel, the sporting director, has written a book on his adventures in the game which included his recollections of the time he kept a penguin in his bath.

As Aberdeen’s hopes for the season have gone down the plughole, he’s not uttered a syllable in public.

Sporting director Lutz Pfannenstiel has had nothing to say during Aberdeen's recent troubles

With former FA bigwig Adrian Bevington now in the chief executive’s role, Pfannenstiel’s input into the recruitment of the new manager will be interesting.

And what role does Dave Cormack have to play? No one doubts the chairman’s passion for the club or his financial commitment to it.

But after seven years in the post, Pittodrie, on his watch, has acquired the unfortunate reputation of being a place where managers crash and burn. And you begin to suspect they might not be the sum total of the issues.

‘Dave Cormack is a close friend and I know he’s done everything in his power to try and get the Dons to a level,’ said McLeish. ‘We saw that Scottish Cup final victory a couple of seasons ago, but the league positions are not what Aberdeen fans expect.

‘Dave will be agonising. You know what it’s like if you get something wrong. You can’t sleep at night.

‘Dave will have been through all that kind of rigmarole as well. There’ll be a big burden on him right now.

‘He’s been stoic and he’s stood by the club. He has to have massive credit for that. It’s just about getting Aberdeen to cement their name back at the higher echelons of Scottish football.’

Alex McLeish was speaking at the announcement of William Hill as official sponsor of Walking Football Scotland. To find your nearest game visit walkingfootballscotland.org

#2059. Michael Morrison reveals major Gianfranco Zola error at Birmingham City

Former Birmingham City captain Michael Morrison has revealed a major error which arose during Gianfranco Zola's ill-fated reign in charge at St. Andrew's @ Knighthead Park between December 2016 and April 2017.

Birmingham are currently a club on the up, with Chris Davies' side topping the Championship for the first time in 18 years over the course of the international break.

Even though Knighthead Capital Management have pumped millions of pounds into various aspects of the club, locals in this part of the Second City haven't lost sight of what was a previously torrid era.

Constant turnover of managers, players and ownership issues were major contributing factors to said demise, with Zola's short time at St. Andrew's epitomising such chaos, as Blues went from potential play-off contenders under to requiring a final-day relegation escape in the same season.

Zola was appointed as Gary Rowett's successor in December 2015 in rather controversial circumstances shortly after a takeover at St. Andrew's from Birmingham Sports Holdings Limited.

Birmingham were seventh in the Championship at the time when the former Chelsea icon and ex-West Ham United and Watford boss signed a two-and-a-half-year contract.

His first match in charge came two days later against high-flying Brighton and Hove Albion in a televised encounter, which Blues lost 2-1 despite taking the lead through Lukas Jutkiewicz.

Featuring on the 'Made in Brum' podcast, Morrison, who made 183 appearances for the club, has revealed that Zola made a glaring error in his first meeting with the players at Wast Hills.

"Sometimes you'd go 'Gianfranco Zola, this is going to be amazing', but in my experience, when you bring them people in, they need to have good people around them," Morrison explained.

"I didn't think he brought in very good staff around him, and that was part of the problem.

"In Gianfranco's first meeting, I remember meeting him and he said 'I'm not going to change too much in a short space of time, we'll see how it goes, we won't be out on the grass too long', and we were out on the grass for two hours, which is a long time to be out before a game, and then we conceded in the last minute.

"Straight away you're like 'you've flogged us there'. If you hadn't had done that, and that's when I talk about wins going by.

"I remember in the first meeting, he's talking about Brighton's team: 'they've got this player, that player. They've got Sam B*****s up front, and we're like 'don't you mean Sam Baldock', who was the Championship's top scorer at the time," the former Blues man added.

"Straight away you're kind of laughing, but if this guy is getting the top scorer wrong on his first day, you think it could be a disaster, and it turned out to be that."

Gary Rowett has revealed how he reacted to his shock sacking at Birmingham City in December 2016

As Morrison alluded to, Zola's reign was nothing short of disastrous, even though Birmingham had the crux of an experienced Championship side and attempted to add further quality in the January window with the additions of Emilio Nsue, Craig Gardner, Cheik Keita and Kerim Frei.

However, Zola won just two of his 24 matches in charge, with it taking 10 of those for the first victory to come against Fulham in February 2017.

Blues fans, where does Gianfranco Zola rank in the club's worst managerial appointments? Comment below!

He then resigned two months later after an Easter Monday defeat to Burton Albion, saying: "It is not that I like quitting, but Birmingham deserves better."

Since then, the Oliena native hasn't returned to management, whilst Harry Redknapp was appointed as his successor, although he, like Zola, would find his St. Andrew's tenure extremely short-lived.

Ultimately, Birmingham continued to go through several managers after Zola's exit, with multiple years of stagnation in the Championship resulting in a relegation to League One in May 2024, less than a year after Knighthead's takeover.

An even costlier decision was made during that particular season too, as John Eustace was sacked in October 2023 despite the fact Birmingham were sixth in the table, with high-profile replacement, Wayne Rooney, lasting less than three months in charge, winning just two of his 15 games.

However, the club's American investors have since learned from their own mistakes by keeping faith in Chris Davies, who has refined his tactical approach this season, and it has certainly got Birmingham fans dreaming of promotion after an extremely encouraging start.

#2058. Blackburn Rovers fans should be excited but worried at new Andri Gudjohnsen exploits

Blackburn have had a positive season under Tony Mowbray, with the side sitting in mid-table going into the international break.

Many had tipped Rovers to be fighting relegation before a ball was kicked this season, but they are just three points away from the play-off places, and they hold a healthy 11-point advantage above 22nd-placed Burnley.

So, all connected to Blackburn will be delighted at how the campaign has begun, and there will be a belief that they can maintain this form, especially due to the form of striker Andri Gudjohnsen.

Whilst it has been a team effort at Rovers, it’s hard not to single out Gudjohnsen, who is the son of former Chelsea man Eidur, for individual praise.

That’s because he has been superb in front of goal, scoring ten times in 14 games, with a shot conversion rate that is better than anyone in Europe, which includes the likes of Raphinha and Erling Haaland.

The 24-year-old is in the form of his career right now, and he continued that on international duty with Iceland on Friday night, as he scored a hat-trick in their 5-0 win at Bulgaria.

This was Gudjohnsen at his devastating best, as he was once again ruthless in front of goal, and it means he now has nine goals in his last six games in all competitions, which is a remarkable return.

Gudjohnsen will hope to extend his goalscoring run when Iceland host Luxembourg on Monday, but, for Blackburn fans, the focus will be on their big game against Burnley when club football returns.

Don Goodman's top three Championship strikers - has he missed anyone out?

This is the game that all Rovers fans would’ve looked for as soon as the fixtures were released, and Mowbray’s men will make the short trip to Turf Moor next weekend.

Burnley were expected to be in the mix for a top two finish this season, but they have had a disastrous season so far, with Nickey Hayen having already lost his job, and the terrible collapse against Cardiff last time out shows the size of the task facing Fabio Celestini.

So, Blackburn will head to their bitter rivals with genuine confidence, and if they are to return home with all three points, you get the feeling that Gudjohnsen will have a significant role to play.

His form, combined with Burnley’s defensive woes, means the striker will fancy his chances of making a difference in the derby.

Of course, Gudjohnsen will be focused on Blackburn when he returns from international duty, and he will be determined to help the team close the gap on the top eight.

However, it’s unrealistic to think that his goalscoring exploits won’t have attracted attention, so the January window could be a challenge for Rovers.

Finding a number nine who can score goals is a major challenge for all clubs, but Gudjohnsen is proving himself to be someone who can do just that.

His ball-striking technique is superb, and Gudjohnsen is proving time and time again that his movement and anticipation is excellent, as he knows how to find space in and around the box.
