You get four guesses per player. Every wrong guess unlocks a clue: first their position, then their nationality, then their initials.

Get it first time for 4 points, then 3, 2 and 1. The best possible score is 20. A new set of players arrives at midnight.

Therefore, it feels inevitable that Rovers’ resolve will be tested in the New Year, but there won’t be any panic at Ewood Park, as the Championship side hold all the cards.

Firstly, there’s no suggestion that the player wants to leave, as Gudjohnsen seems to be enjoying his football, and he will recognise now Mowbray’s appointment has helped his game.

Plus, he signed a new ‘long-term’ contract earlier this year, so Blackburn won’t be in a position where they have to cash in.

But, that’s a discussion for January, and the only concern for Blackburn will be beating Burnley on Sunday, with Gudjohnsen having the chance to make himself even more of a hero to the supporters if he scores the winner at Turf Moor.

#2057. ‘Embarrassing themselves!’ Jamie Carragher hits back at Rio Ferdinand over Cristiano Ronaldo defence

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Jamie Carragher has taken aim at Rio Ferdinand for defending Cristiano Ronaldo following the Portuguese forward’s latest controversy with his national team.

Ronaldo has found himself at the centre of a growing dispute after abruptly leaving Portugal’s national team camp following a reported disagreement with head coach Jorge Jesus. The incident has prompted strong reactions from former teammates and pundits, with Ferdinand firmly siding with the Al-Nassr star.

Jamie Carragher, however, believes Ronaldo’s reputation should not shield him from criticism when his behaviour becomes difficult to defend.

Rio Ferdinand, who played alongside Cristiano Ronaldo at Manchester United between 2003 and 2009, criticised Jorge Jesus for the way he handled the situation.

Speaking on his Rio Presents podcast, the former United captain criticised Jesus for not showing respect to Cristiano Ronaldo.

“Cristiano Ronaldo was put in a very bad situation and was treated in an inappropriate way. He has an enormous history in football. Jorge Jesus did not deal with Ronaldo well and chose to treat him in that way, but I cannot find any explanation for it,” Ferdinand said.

Despite Jesus being Portugal’s manager, Ferdinand questioned his standing, comparing him with the likes of Sir Alex Ferguson and Pep Guardiola. To say the least, Ferdinand only embarrassed himself doing that.

Jamie Carragher saw the argument very differently and rightfully so.

Speaking on The Overlap Daily, the former Liverpool defender suggested that Ferdinand’s friendship with Ronaldo was influencing his reaction to the incident.

“People think it’s good to be close to people in the game, but this is where it becomes a problem,” Carragher explained.

He then called out both Ferdinand and broadcaster Piers Morgan, who has also been vocal in his support of Ronaldo.

“People like Piers Morgan and Rio Ferdinand, who are big people in the media whether you like them or not, are embarrassing themselves with the nonsense that they’re coming out with because they know Ronaldo. You can know someone and try to look after them, but you cannot defend the indefensible.”

Carragher believes Ronaldo’s achievements should not put the forward beyond criticism, particularly when his actions involve his national team and its management.

Gary Neville, who also played with Cristiano Ronaldo, has questioned Ronaldo’s handling of the situation, despite his longstanding affection for his former Manchester United teammate.

Neville argued that Ronaldo’s public response had made matters worse and the forward needed to accept criticism rather than expect others to defend him.

“With Cristiano, it’s difficult because I love him, but I can’t watch what happened at United in his second period and then see him make a statement like that without saying something. He has to be pulled up on that and told that it isn’t acceptable,” Neville said.

He also suggested Ronaldo’s extraordinary success, wealth and influence could have contributed to a sense that he was beyond challenge.

“When you have a billion pounds in the bank, the achievements he has had and the power he has, you feel you’re unbreakable, you feel you’re unstoppable,” Neville added.

Despite his criticism, Neville does not believe the episode will define Ronaldo’s legacy permanently. He expects the attention to fade once the forward retires, allowing his achievements on the pitch to take centre stage again.

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

#2056. Journey to Wembley: Halifax set sights on winning more fans and climbing mountain to the Football League as their FA Cup adventure is ended by route-one Cheltenham

By IAN HERBERT AT EV CHARGER POINTS STADIUM

Published: 21:00 EST, 8 November 2026 | Updated: 18:05 EST, 9 November 2026

The FC Halifax manager John McGrath’s father-in-law is the top British mountaineer Alan Hinkes OBE and he knows that he has something of a summit of his own to scale, as he walks straight from the team bus to the pitch, set in the lee of Cheltenham’s mist-shrouded Cleeve Hill.

McGrath is all-too familiar with this setting. He brought his Buxton team here in the FA Cup's second round last season and was on the fringes of a major upset – 2-1 up at half-time – before watching the game fall away.

Cheltenham are a much stronger club, 12 months on, but our few weeks with McGrath, an Irishman who 25 years ago was playing for Aston Villa against Liverpool in the Premier League, reveal his ambition for the National League side he joined this summer.

We’re alongside him and FC Halifax on our journey to Wembley, told through all 13 rounds, following a club as far as they go and then picking up with the team who knock them out, all the way to the final.

A mist-veiled evening at Halifax’s Shay Stadium, a few weeks back, revealed much about 46-year-old McGrath. He arrives with his two-man coaching team for a Q&A with fans who, when the evening’s proceedings are finished, form a long and orderly queue to talk to him, one-on-one. The team’s four-hour drive to Southend awaits the following morning. But McGrath has no eye for the clock.

This managerial journey is one he hadn’t anticipated when, aged 32, a serious foot injury ended his long career with Burton Albion. He secured a master’s degree in broadcasting and media at Staffordshire University and had begun spots for Sky Sports and BBC local radio, yet here he is on ‘the grass’ he'd never wanted to leave.

His wife Fiona is Director of Public Realm at Islington Council and their time together is some weeks limited to a coffee at Doncaster station, near their home, when he drops her before daybreak. ‘It’s our little half an hour,’ he relates. ‘We’re ships that pass in the night.'

Cheltenham edged out FC Halifax 2-0 in front of 2,961 fans to reach round two

Cheltenham boss Steve Cotterill shakes hands with Halifax manager John McGrath

Ground staff put up the goalposts at EV Charger Points Stadium, also known as Whaddon Road

His club’s fans are partisan – 350 are here – and yearn for League Two, though there’s not a fortune at McGrath’s disposal. The club’s owner David Bosomworth was at the club when it went bust in 2008 and they say that experience left him painfully aware of the need for sustainability. Fair enough. Bosomworth has declined to speak to us. He is not at Cheltenham.

An hour out from kick-off, McGrath is handing out training vests for the warm-up as well as observing things – it’s a modest backroom team – and that defeat last season perhaps plays on his mind. ‘Cheltenham tweaked it that day and personally, I think I should have done the same,’ he says. ‘I’m a better manager, 12 months on.’

The club could badly use the £50,000 prize money that a win here would bring, not to mention the accompanying shot of exhilaration.

McGrath already belongs in the margins of the tournament’s history. He and his son, Jay, a Doncaster Rovers player, became the first father and son to play in the same team in an FA Cup game when McGrath, Mickleover Sports' player-manager for their preliminary-round win against Coventry Sphinx in 2019, fielded his 17-year-old boy.

Halifax seem up for this challenge. Their players go looking for their kit in their bus's luggage compartment before the driver has jumped out to produce the key. But Cheltenham have come a long way in the past year.

The former Burnley owner Mike Garlick bought the club 15 months ago and his investment strategy has echoes of the methods which made the Turf Moor club a Premier League side for seven out of eight years. Manager Steve Cotterill, quickly installed by Garlick, was briefly with him at Burnley.

Cheltenham fans arrive at the ground with their side riding high, fifth in League Two

Halifax fans cheer on their side but their Cup run is over - they travel to Kidderminster next Saturday in the National League

Mike Garlick bought Cheltenham last year and quickly installed Cotterill as manager

There’s an air of optimism around the place, as head groundsman Paul Gibson attends to some stray grass cuttings with a leaf blower and husband and wife team Gerry and Hannah Oldham sort the kit.

The ground staff, with their distinctive West Country burr, take enjoyment from several attempts at installing the goalposts before getting them upright. Mascot ‘Whaddney the Robin’ is limbering up for a leisurely lap of the stadium.

Garlick suggests we use Cotterill’s office as a quiet place to talk as the stadium begins to fill up. He tries calling the manager for his consent to do so but Cotterill’s phone rings out in the room.

It turns out Cotterill is receiving calls on his watch. This is a source of some humour as Cotterill eventually barrels into the office, exhibiting his usual energy. The two men clearly get on.

Extra preliminary round, August 8: Nelson 3-2 Stockport Georgians

Preliminary round, August 22: Witton Albion 1-0 Nelson

1st qualifying round, September 5: Avro 0-2 Witton Albion

2nd qualifying round, September 19: South Shields 7-0 Witton Albion

3rd qualifying round, October 3: Buxton 0-2 South Shields

4th qualifying round, October 17: Halifax 3-2 South Shields

1st round, November 7: Cheltenham Town 2-0 Halifax

Garlick looks more at home here than at Burnley. He also owns Spanish third-tier side Antequera CF and a club in Gibraltar but yearned for this kind of environment and Cheltenham, tumbling towards the EFL exit last year, badly needed help.

‘I missed English football,’ Garlick says. ‘I thought this was a club where I could make a difference and have some impact. When I came in, we had four points from nine games and were bottom of the league. It was hard work last season, but the fans seem happy for me to be here.

'Everyone who comes up to me says “thanks for helping the club,” which is nice. I never quite got that at Burnley to be honest. I’ve probably got more thanks here in 12 months than in 17 years on the board at Burnley. But that’s football isn’t it?!’

There’s been substantial investment in the training ground, a new stadium hospitality lounge, and Cotterill’s contacts have contributed to some eye-catching signings including veteran former Villa forward Andreas Weimann from Rapid Vienna.

Halifax start the game well, with wingers Ben Tollitt and Connor Douglas both flying and Conor Carty, the Irish forward who has been a revelation this season, ready to receive. Twice, they go close in the early stages. But Cotterill has a very uncomplicated route-one method in place and whip-fast striker Kirsten Otchere, on loan from Leicester City, looks lethal.

Will Flint’s string of fine interventions for Halifax is matched by the excellence of goalkeeper True Grant but Otchere crosses from the left for Jake Evans to score, left-footed.

This time McGrath does change things, adjusting the defensive line to quell Otchere’s threat after half time. Cotterill, whose colourful touchline antics include shoving his full-back out of the way when he blocks his line of view, grows concerned as Cheltenham’s attacking threat fades and Halifax dominate.

Carty nets from a Douglas’ crosses but is offside. Halifax lack the moment of class to convert superiority into goals. Substitute George Miller’s late header ended Halifax’s tournament for another year.

Halifax's Ben Tollitt battles for possession but his side cannot find a way through Cheltenham

McGrath and his Halifax players applaud the travelling supporters after their Cup exit

The Cheltenham fans flood out into suburban Whaddon Road fortified in their belief that something is building. ‘An FA Cup run is great for morale and also a massive contribution to the finances, because we’re hopefully going to strengthen again in January and the summer,’ says Garlick.

A foggy trip up the M6 awaits Halifax, though McGrath brings both a hinterland and perspective to the quest of start filling the famous old Shay Stadium once more and pushing closer to the fourth tier after 24 years away. His father-in-law was the first Briton to climb all the world's 8,000m mountains, in the Himalayan and Karakoram ranges of Nepal, Tibet and Pakistan.

‘He’s one of the best in the world,’ McGrath relates. ‘And however tough it is being a footballer or football manager, you try sitting on the side of Mount Everest for three months.

‘There’s a rumour building in our town that we play good football, that there’s goals to be seen at our stadium and a bunch of talented young men are representing the club in the right way.

'We want to get another 800 fans a week through the door and kick the club up the division and into the Football League. We think we can do that.’

#2055. All 24 EFL Championship clubs ranked by how successful they've been in the last 25 years

There has been a lot of success to go around the 24 teams currently competing in the Championship over the last 25 years.

What defines success to a club is different for everyone, and the wider context of a team’s history can make a National League title more significant than coming second in a higher division.

From Birmingham City to Wrexham, all of the teams in the second division this year have achieved a piece of history for their club.

But not all success is created equal, from winning the EFL and FA Cups, to simply achieving promotion through the play-offs.

Here we rank the 24 Championship sides based on how successful they’ve been since 2001/02…

Blackburn Rovers won the Premier League title in 1995, but that was over 30 years ago now, and they have yet to come close to that kind of success since.

In just the last 25 years, the Lancashire outfit have a second place in League One in 2018 as their greatest success, with their highest league finish being sixth in the Premier League in 2003 and 2006.

Preston North End were one of the most successful clubs in the very early days of organised football in the UK.

However, the Lilywhites have not many major achievements to celebrate over the last 25 years, with their greatest success in this period being their 2015 play-off victory in League One.

Stoke City came close to achieving the greatest success in their history in 2011 when they lost the FA Cup final to Manchester City.

Instead, the Potters have had to settle for play-off victory as their greatest moment of success in the last 25 years, earning promotion to the second tier in 2002.

Millwall came close to automatic promotion to the Premier League in 2025, but had to settle for the play-offs, where they lost to Hull City in the semi-finals.

But they have achieved play-off success in the last 25 years, twice winning the competition at a League One level (2010 & 2017).

Charlton Athletic were competing in the Premier League 25 years ago, and have fallen to League One in the time since.

But their 2012 title victory in the third tier remains an impressive achievement, and they have also won the play-offs at that level twice in this period (2019 & 2025).

Watford won the Championship play-offs in 2006 under Aidy Boothroyd to earn their place in the Premier League.

But their closest brush with real success came in the 2019 FA Cup final, where they lost to Manchester City in a 6-0 defeat.

Southampton have brushed up against big success in the last 25 years, reaching both an FA Cup and EFL Cup final in this period, as well as coming second in the Championship in 2011.

But their greatest achievement since 2001 was their 2024 play-off final victory, which earned Russell Martin’s side a place in the Premier League.

Bolton Wanderers’ decline from Premier League side to League Two outfit occurred within the last 25 years, which meant a lot of difficult campaigns for supporters.

FLW have looked at the individuals who are currently taking home the second tier's biggest wages

The most recent season saw Steven Schumacher’s side win the League One play-offs, which may be their greatest achievement of this entire period, although the 2023 EFL Trophy victory is also a moment fans will remember fondly for years to come.

Wrexham spent several seasons competing at a non-league level during this 25-year period, but they’ve enjoyed a remarkable rise under their Hollywood ownership in the few campaigns.

The Red Dragons’ greatest success in this timeframe was their National League title victory in 2023, which they won with a staggering 111 points, before going on to earn two more automatic promotions in the following two years.

Derby County’s greatest successes all came in the post-World War years, with the high watermark being two league titles in the 1970s.

But over the last 25 years, the Rams’ biggest achievement was their 2007 play-off final victory under Billy Davies, which earned them a place in the Premier League.

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

#2054. Is Mainoo-Tielemans the right midfield option for Man Utd?

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United drew 1-1 at home to Tottenham despite having an extra man advantage. The Reds saw Mbeumo put them in front, but Solanke scored a late leveller in injury time after Bentancur was shown a red card for headbutting Luke Shaw.

Manchester United’s worrisome season continues after accumulating just six points off the first six games, their first start in 40 years. But more than the results, what worries Manchester United is their midfield pairing.

United spent massively on their midfield rebuild and somehow can’t find the best combination in the midfield. Michael Carrick has gone with the Mainoo-Tielemans partnership, but they have failed to control the midfield. Individually, they both come with decent numbers, but collectively they fall short.

Mainoo and Tielemans struggled against Tottenham while closing the midfield and at times showed a lack of energy. On the day when nothing clicked for United, the Mainoo-Tielemans partnership particularly raised eyebrows.

Gary Neville has also raised serious concerns about Manchester United’s Mainoo-Tielemans partnership.

Neville was particularly unimpressed by the midfield balance, arguing that the problems went beyond one disappointing performance.

The former United captain stopped short of ruling out the combination altogether, but he suggested he has serious doubts about its suitability.

“Tielemans and Mainoo, I’m not calling time on it, that’s probably too drastic, but I’ve seen enough of it to think that’s not a midfield that’s dynamic enough to play at this football club in the Premier League.”

Former Spurs midfielder Redknapp felt United struggled to connect their midfield with the rest of the team during the opening stages.

He added: “I didn’t see enough connections in that midfield in the first hour. Baleba came on and did OK and showed a bit more.

“They look devoid of any confidence, any idea, any patterns of play, playing out from the back. Tottenham were much better than them in every single department.”

While Carlos Baleba was a particular highlight on the day, he showed great potential when brought on. He brought a new sense of energy in the midfield and sent a few balls over the top for a couple of quick counterattacks as well.

Redknapp also pointed to the physical demands of modern Premier League football, arguing that United cannot afford to be outworked by their opponents.

Tottenham ran a staggering 5 km more than Manchester United, something Redknapp felt was a pattern.

"Spurs ran 5km more than Man United… it's like that EVERY GAME!"Jamie Redknapp is less than impressed with the lack of ground covered this season by Manchester United 😬 pic.twitter.com/BxWkFuyGJ1

“There is a pattern that I don’t really like when I watch teams, and that’s when every time the stats don’t lie and the other team runs more than you.

“I feel in Premier League football right now there are a couple of non-negotiables, and one is you have to run more than the other team if you want to win or at least get close enough. Tonight Spurs ran 5km more than Manchester United.”

He believes Tottenham’s pressing exposed the lack of pace in United’s midfield, making it difficult for the hosts to move the ball safely through the centre.

“In terms of how they played today, I think they have an issue with Tielemans and Mainoo in midfield. I don’t see enough energy in there. Baleba will add a lot and will give them a tremendous amount of energy but right now, when things aren’t going well… and Tottenham knew it!

“Every time United played the ball out from the back they pounced. They pressed them because they knew United don’t have the pace and speed in midfield to get away.”

“In isolation, one of them is absolutely fine, the two of them together doesn’t work for me. They haven’t got the pace or the energy to get around the pitch for 90 minutes.”

#2053. One lesson Senne Lammens learned after Tottenham’s late equaliser

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Senne Lammens believes Manchester United must learn to finish games off after their late collapse against Tottenham Hotspur.

The result left the goalkeeper frustrated at Old Trafford despite leading the game and with a player more on the field.

Manchester United looked set for three points after Bryan Mbeumo put them ahead in the second half, but Dominic Solanke’s stoppage-time equaliser denied Michael Carrick’s side a much-needed victory.

Speaking to MUTV, Senne Lammens admitted the team had struggled early on but had done enough after the break to put themselves in a winning position.

“Football can be a bit cruel at times,” the Belgian goalkeeper said. “There are a lot of ups and downs, especially in a game like that.”

Senne Lammens felt United failed to take advantage of Tottenham’s red card, allowing the visitors too much space to find a late equaliser.

“We’ve won a lot of games against 10 men [in the past], but you also know there can be a tricky situation when you’re playing against 10 men, because they have nothing to lose and they know that.”

He added that United should have scored again to put the result beyond doubt or, at the very least, prevented Tottenham from creating further chances.

“I think that’s what we did wrong. We gave them a little bit too much breathing room to create those chances, and then one ball has to go right for them, one bounce has to go their way, and they can score like that.”

The goalkeeper also pointed to similarities with United’s earlier disappointment against Everton, where points slipped away late in the match.

“You feel like those games just slip away from you, and there are one or two situations where you don’t really have control,” he said.

“I think there’s a lot to learn from today. It wasn’t all negative, not at all, but especially the way it ended left a very bad feeling.”

“I think the easy answer is that we have to be able to kill the game faster and not put ourselves in that position to give them a chance.”

Despite the frustration, Lammens said there were positives to take from the performance. However, the late goal had left a bitter feeling, and United now need to turn those lessons into better results.

Heading into the international break, Manchester United occupy one of the bottom three places, with only Crystal Palace and Sunderland beneath the Red Devils as things stand, with just one win in their opening ten Premier League games.

Michael Carrick has been dismissed from his post at Old Trafford, as it appears as though former Chelsea and Tottenham Hotspur head coach Mauricio Pochettino will leave his post as the USMNT boss to head to the English North West to save the global giants.

Manchester City, meanwhile, sit 3rd in the Premier League right now beneath Brighton & Hove Albion and Arsenal. With seemingly no chance of relegation based on that, the Citizens await the verdict on the outcome after being found guilty of 114 of the 115 charges brought against them relating to financial wrongdoing and non-cooperation.

A heavy punishment is almost certainly heading their way, with expulsion or a heavy points deduction being bandied about, as Football League World takes a look at what the 2027/28 EFL Championship table would look like, according to AI, if both Manchester clubs suffered the drop.

#2051. Manchester United defence: Why Carrick must trust Yoro and Heaven

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United endured another embarrassing draw against 10-man Tottenham after failing to register their second win despite bringing in a plethora of players.

Manchester United invested heavily last season in their attacking options, bringing in Bryan Mbeumo and Matheus Cunha.

This season, they overhauled their midfield. United spent massively on Carlos Baleba, Youri Tielemans and Andrey Santos. Despite spending massively, they have failed to produce the control they would have expected in the midfield. The lack of control was evident in the recent Tottenham game, where Mainoo-Tielemans partnership failed to control the game collectively.

The midfield question was covered in our recent report as well, highlighting a lack of control and carried comments from Gary Neville and Jamie Redknapp.

The bigger question is when United will strengthen their defence. As per our previous report as well, Manchester United are planning to overhaul their defence in the upcoming transfer windows.

United were heavily linked with several left backs but eventually failed to bring any one. The situation left Luke Shaw as the only senior option in the position, while Patrick Dorgu is used upfront by Michael Carrick.

Harry Amass is another option for Michael Carrick, but he is only here because of a failed transfer move. He has been rarely used at left back as Dalot and Mazraoui get the nod in the position ahead of him.

The situation at right-back isn’t all good either. Diogo Dalot is the first option there, but he rarely provides the assurance that puts Michael Carrick at ease. Noussair Mazraoui is the second choice at right-back, but his attacking numbers aren’t great either.

Harry Maguire and Lisandro Martinez form up United’s centre-back pairing while Leny Yoro and Ayden Heaven are used all over the defensive line in rotation when needed. Yoro was used at right-back against Tottenham while Heaven arrived as left-back towards the end of the game.

Manchester United’s season hasn’t been defensively strong or anywhere near being good. They have conceded goals in all Premier League games and only kept one clean sheet against Sabah in the Champions League.

Lisandro Martinez has come under scrutiny after errors on four occasions. Against Ipswich Town, Manchester United won 5-2, but his error resulted in Ipswich’s second goal.

His own goal against Fulham gave away two points at Craven Cottage in the 1-1 draw. Another error led to Solanke’s goal against Tottenham, when he took his eyes off the ball.

His injury concerns are another aspect, and maybe Michael Carrick needs to make a decision.

Manchester United’s success depended heavily on youth over the years. When pundits questioned them, Sir Alex Ferguson gave them the chance to prove them wrong. Alan Hansen made the famous quote “You can’t win anything with kids” on the BBC’s Match of the Day on August 19, 1995. United did exactly that, played youth and went on to win everything.

Michael Carrick needs to do exactly that. He needs to trust youth and allow them to make mistakes. Leny Yoro and Ayden Heaven arrived as massive potentials, but they haven’t been given the license to express themselves, without fearing for their places. Both the youngsters have rarely started together or in a stretch of games. It is maybe time for them to take their chances.

Michael Carrick needs to keep Harry Maguire as a senior experienced figure around them. There is no doubt over Martinez’s ability, but he is not the answer to United’s long-term problem. United need to blend youth with experience as their identity moving forward. Lisandro Martinez perhaps isn’t the answer to United’s problem.

A defensive revival expected in the upcoming transfer windows needs to happen now. It must happen with chances to the Youth. Michael Carrick needs to be brave enough and throw the youngsters in the mix. What it will bring is a long-term solution. They need to experience the errors and pressures that will shape them in football.

Carrick’s achievements last season bought him the permanent role, but the opening weeks of this campaign have left little room for further slip-ups.

#2049. Mikkel Ladefoged is Lorent Tolaj 2.0 for Lincoln City

Lincoln City star-power recruit Mikkel Ladefoged has the potential to be the difference maker for the Imps this season, and has a blueprint to copy.

The side jointly-managed by Tom Shaw and Chris Cohen might have lost their most recent three games, but have four wins in the Championship next to their name, including an impressive 4-1 victory over Burnley.

Lincoln sit five points above the drop zone after almost a third of the season. That is a good start for a team promoted last term, heavily tipped to return straight back to League One, and under somewhat new management.

Michael Skubala is under-fire at Bristol City, having jumped ship from the Imps after completing a historic and impressive title win. That left Shaw and Cohen to lead Lincoln, having been elevated from their backroom staff positions.

Lincoln City’s return to the Championship has been 65 years in the making, and they have prepared for it in a risky way.

And, they have done that admirably to date. Avoiding relegation has to be the aim for the Lincolnshire outfit, and they have started well enough to sit outside of the dreaded drop zone. A key part of that early success has been the strikers who made the difference last season.

Ben House (against Bolton Wanderers) and Freddie Draper (against both Preston North End and Bristol City) scored the only goals of 1-0 wins. But, there is a more headline-grabbing attacker who has begun to make a difference too.

Signed by the Imps in the summer for a club record fee, Ladefoged left Swedish outfit Vasteras SK after netting 19 goals in 29 games for his previous club. The fee is believed to be beyond that of the £2.5million paid for commanding Romanian international centre-back Andrei Coubis.

With the hefty price tag, relative to the club’s previous deals, Ladefoged came with high hopes and a large weight rested on his shoulders for the relegation fight. He has shown enough already to prove himself as the key to potential Lincoln success.

The 6’8” Dane took a while to get his first league start, but marked it with a brace against Burnley. He followed that up with an assist in defeat to Birmingham City, and has looked a real threat at the top end of the pitch.

Mikkel Ladefoged career stats by club (credit: Fotmob)

Across nine appearances, he has three goals, netting a late equaliser against Southampton off the bench in the weeks after his signing. The 23-year-old’s height, physicality, pace, and decision-making make him the perfect striker for Lincoln and their quest for continued Championship status.

He has the potential to score plenty more as well, having shown his willingness to create chances for his teammates as well as scoring his own.

In 2010-11 Adel Taarabt ran Championship defences ragged as QPR won the title. Now it’s your turn: weave him past cones, lunging defenders and flying slide tackles, and get the ball in the net at the end of each level. There are 15 levels and only three lives. How far can you take him?

A Football League World arcade game. Best played with sound on.

He fits the mould of Lorent Tolaj. The 25-year-old Swiss striker has found goals hard to come by since being elevated to the Championship this summer. He signed for Skubala’s Bristol City after a spell-binding campaign at Plymouth Argyle.

In League One, Tolaj found the net 18 times across 33 games, being only three others in the top scorer charts. Considering he missed a portion of the latter stages of the season due to injury, it is an impressive return.

It has been a fast rise for Tolaj, scoring 25 in a season in the National League, then recording 24 goal involvements the following term in League Two before his move to Plymouth.

He does not match Ladefoged for height, as very few do, but his pace and power are a real comparison. Tolaj finds space in tight pockets in and around the box, shifts through seemingly too narrow alleys to create dangerous shooting opportunities.

Ladefoged is a similar archetype, and could propel well beyond Tolaj given his additional qualities and early signs in a Lincoln shirt.

Simply put, if the Imps can keep Ladefoged firing and craft a unit that suits his strengths, then relegation will be very little of a concern come the end of the Championship campaign.

#2047. Why must Manchester United move beyond the Glazers?

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

The Glazers took control of Manchester United in 2005. That takeover has since caused so many disruptions and protests by the fans over massive debts and insufficient backing of the team in the transfer market, despite successful periods in the Premier League and Europe.

Now Manchester United need to think about who should control the club in the future. Laurie Whitwell has now shared his views on why Manchester United need an ownership change. He simply wants the Glazers gone from Old Trafford. However, he also recognizes that replacing the current ownership doesn’t automatically mean things will improve for United.

The journalist believes the change in ownership has had a lasting effect on United, with the club still feeling its consequences today. A fresh start might be welcome, but the identity of the next owners would matter just as much as the departure of the existing ones.

“Personally, I would like to see the Glazers gone from Manchester United,” Laurie Whitwell said on The Rest is Football podcast with Gary Lineker.

“That change, you know, just really affected and still impacts United now.”

The desire for different ownership is one thing, but finding people capable of taking the club forward is another.

Whitwell admitted there is no perfect answer when it comes to who should take charge.

That leaves United facing a difficult choice. A sale could offer the chance to change how the club is run, but simply bringing in a new owner would not guarantee better decisions or a brighter future.

Whitwell also raised the idea of United supporters having a greater say in the club’s ownership and decisions, much like the Barcelona model where fans own the club itself. However, he highlighted that the Barcelona model isn’t ideal either and has its own shortcomings.

Changing the names at the top would only be one part of the change. The club would also need an ownership structure capable of making sound decisions and providing a direction.

The issue of ownership goes beyond whether the Glazers should stay or leave. Manchester United needs to find the right people and the right model to secure the club’s future. Getting that decision wrong could leave them facing the same circus the fans are enduring under the Glazers.

#2046. Barry Bannan to change career? Sheffield Wednesday star’s exploits have caused a reaction

Sheffield Wednesday are rebuilding themselves as a club and institution on the pitch this season but their rebuild goes deeper and further than that off it, needing to reconnect with supporters and get back in touch with their history.

Having gone into administration last season, Sheffield Wednesday eventually succumbed to an inevitable relegation, albeit a defeat of West Bromwich Albion on the final day of the season at Hillsborough did help them finish the season on zero points rather than in the minuses.

The Owls have since started this season in a mixed manner, leading to the departure of manager Henrik Pedersen and the subsequent appointment of Dave Challinor.

Such was the mess the club had got into, on-pitch improvement would naturally always have to be subject to so-called ‘teething issues’, while the off-the-pitch commercial aspect of the club also needed improvement.

As a part of that potential rebuild off the pitch and increased commercialisation of the club as it seeks to reconnect with supporters, Wednesday have launched a collaboration with esteemed artist Pete McKee.

60-year-old McKee, born in Sheffield and a cartoonist for the Sheffield Telegraph, has built his artistic reputation on depicting working class elements of society and specifically northern culture – and even more specifically, Sheffield culture.

Within this new collaboration, it has been launched at Riverside Fish and Chip shop on Langsett Road in Hillsborough, nearby to the famous Hillsborough Tap pub and just around the corner from the home ground of Sheffield Wednesday.

FLW’s Sheffield Wednesday fan pundit has reacted to yet another crushing injury blow to defender Max Lowe.

As a part of the launch, club legend, or at the very least cult hero, Barry Bannan has featured in the photoshoot dressed as a chippy worker behind the counter.

The actual collaboration centres on the launch of a range of clothing such as hoodies and t-shirts with McKee’s Wednesday-focused art as the design.

McKee has explained: “I’ve supported Wednesday ever since I was a nipper, with my two elder brothers taking it in turns to take me until I was old enough to go by myself with my mates.

“The club has been a part of my DNA throughout my life and career, so it’s with immense pride that I now have the opportunity to work directly with the club on this range.

“I hope the fans take it to their hearts, as well all share the same love of following the Wednesday.”

Naturally, there has been quite the response from Sheffield Wednesday supporters to the comically produced involvement of Bannan in the photoshoot.

Many have expressed their amusement with the 36-year-old, soon to be 37, getting involved to such a degree with The Owls’ social media post nudging him to ensure he doesn’t give up his ‘day job’.

However, given the mixed performances of Bannan on the pitch, one Wednesday supporter did quip that perhaps the Scotsman may be best suited to the role he has been pictured in.

Having spent nine and a half years at Hillsborough, making over 475 appearances for the club in his first stint in South Yorkshire, Bannan moved to Millwall in January.

More of a bit-part player for The Lions as they finished third in the Championship before losing to eventual play-off winners Hull City in the play-off semi-final, Bannan returned from the capital city with Wednesday set to relaunch themselves as an entity this summer and this season.

18 new signings came through the door, including Bannan, and there were some standout performances and moments in the early couple of months, such as a 7-2 victory over Bromley and a 5-0 shellacking of AFC Wimbledon.

Inconsistency in terms of performances and results getting worse on the pitch, especially away from home, led to the departure of Henrik Pedersen with former Stockport County boss Dave Challinor now tasked with ensuring a title challenge in League One.

That inconsistency and general level of frustration felt by supporters has perhaps best been represented by the dip of Bannan’s own level; not quite as protective of possession nor as dynamic with or without the ball as he was in the past.

The arrival of Challinor could and perhaps should spark a Wednesday surge – and there will have to be improvement from their still talismanic figure in the middle of the park to help that along.

#2044. 3 in, 3 out: How Birmingham City's dream January transfer window looks

Birmingham City are flying high in the Championship at present, with Chris Davies' side leading the division across the November international break.

Blues are ahead of Middlesbrough and Stoke City as a result of their superior goal difference after 15 matches, with it now remaining to be seen if they can match or better such performance levels.

Such feats come after an interesting summer of activity, during which fewer additions and sales were made at St. Andrew's @ Knighthead Park than in years gone by, whilst newly-appointed director of football, John Murtough, is set to oversee his first recruitment drive.

With that said, Football League World have looked at what an ideal January window could look like for Birmingham amid their promotion hopes.

Getting things underway would be an attempted pursuit for FC Lorient's Arthur Avom, with FLW exclusively revealing Birmingham's interest in the 21-year-old alongside that of Middlesbrough.

Despite being relatively young, Avom's impressive performances for the French side have attracted the attention of several Premier League sides, as well as other top-flight sides across Europe, with valuations as high as £17.8m touted in the meantime.

He hasn't let such speculation impact his early-season performances for Lorient either, and with his contract expiring next June, Birmingham could eye a potential cut-price deal for his services, should Avom's current employers not seek to tie him down to fresh terms in the coming weeks.

Another cut-price deal which may arise for Davies and his recruitment team is one for Canadian international, Liam Millar, who has certainly made his mark already at St. Andrew's on loan from Hull City.

Blues fended off interest from the two sides directly below them in the table to land the winger's services for the season, and after netting for the Tigers in the Premier League against Coventry, Millar has since produced four goals and three assists in his 11 Championship appearances so far this term.

Those in East Yorkshire have also been left confused by the 27-year-old's recent admissions in interviews, and whilst Hull have the option to extend his contract until June 2028, it could make sense for all parties to wrap up a permanent deal as early as January.

Birmingham's two main attackers, August Priske and Jay Stansfield, have caused plenty of havoc for defences so far this season, but there are question marks beyond the duo.

Having been linked with a return last January, as well as being the subject of interest from local rivals, Wolves, in the summer, Birmingham could explore a reunion with Scotland international, Che Adams, seven years after his departure for Southampton.

However, how feasible this deal would be remains to be seen, as the experienced striker is now in his third season with Torino, and has already scored four goals in his first nine Serie A outings this season under Ignazio Abate.

Middlesbrough's Kim Hellberg and Swansea City's Vitor Matos are on the radar of Crystal Palace, which could be good news for their promotion rivals.

Lee Buchanan has become rather forgotten about at St. Andrew's, with the last two years being a tale of injury misfortune for the Birmingham defender.

The 25-year-old hasn't featured competitively for 23 months, with a serious knee injury followed by further setbacks, and he is now firmly behind Alex Cochrane and Kristoffer Lund in the pecking order.

Buchanan attracted interest from Luton Town and Reading in the summer, and it would be no surprise to see transfer links surround him in January, with it appearing that an exit could be the best decision for his long-term career.

Marvin Ducksch's immediate future at St. Andrew's has been up in the air for some time, and it wouldn't be the greatest of surprises to see last summer's £1.75m arrival find a new club this winter.

Ducksch hit double-figures last season, but after being charged for causing a three-vehicle collision in April, the two-time German international has been the subject of heavy exit talk.

Blues fans, will Marvin Ducksch stay or go this January? Comment below!

Indeed, it was suggested that the forward came mightily close to joining Valencia on Deadline Day before a deal between the two clubs collapsed, and since then, he has started in none of his nine Championship appearances so far this season.

Scott Wright was recently inserted back into Birmingham's 25-man squad following Kanya Fujimoto's loan move to Tokyo Verdy, but a swift departure for the 29-year-old winger also seems inevitable.

After featuring heavily in all competitions two seasons ago after signing from Rangers, Wright suffered a serious injury against Reading last February, and only made a full recovery in the latter weeks of 2025.

Since then, an underwhelming loan with Dundee FC and limited minutes with Blues have followed, meaning that if the Scottish youth international wants to source regular minutes, it would be elsewhere rather than being behind Carlos Vicente and Patrick Roberts in Davies' pecking order.

#2041. 'I've never revealed the whole story': England fans wouldn't boo me if they knew the truth, says Ben White... as he defends World Cup walkout and rejecting call-ups

Published: 15:00 EST, 9 November 2026 | Updated: 18:20 EST, 9 November 2026

Ben White believes England fans would not boo him if they knew the truth behind his 2022 World Cup walkout.

The Arsenal defender left the camp in Qatar after the group stages citing 'personal reasons', although a fallout with Gareth Southgate's assistant, Steve Holland, was later reported. He then declined a return to the England set-up while Southgate and Holland were in charge but was included by Thomas Tuchel in March of this year.

However, White was booed by fans inside Wembley when he came on in the second-half of a friendly against Uruguay, only to score his first international goal and then concede a late penalty in a 1-1 draw. He was not selected for the World Cup in North America but is back with the squad ahead of this week's Nations League fixtures against Croatia and Spain.

And the 29-year-old, addressing those jeers for the first time, said: 'It was hard to hear, especially because they didn't know the situation, which is kind of what I put my mind to.

'They don't know my situation and it is not really about what I have done, because they don't know why, and if they did they would not be booing or saying these things. I just think about maybe (them) not knowing the full story, and if they did, maybe they wouldn't (boo).'

He added: 'Obviously, a load has been said. I've probably seen all of it. I just think people don't know the full story, because I've never said anything, and I won't put anyone under the bus or share any of that.

Ben White has insisted he would not be booed by England fans if they knew the truth behind his World Cup walkout

White fell out with former England assistant Steve Holland (left) before heading home during the 2022 World Cup

'There were loads of things that happened and communication wasn't great. I don't want to throw anyone under the bus. A lot of people try to do that to me so it's something I wouldn't want to do myself. I think it's probably the best way. It's a bit tricky and not straightforward, but it's amazing to be here now. It's a privilege to be called up.'

White, however, is adamant that he would make the same decision today and leave the World Cup, even if he will not divulge the reasons as to why.

'I don't think I would (do anything different),' he said. 'I think, as a person, I'm quite real, and I made the decision that I would probably continue to make. It's one that I'm not happy about. It wasn't ideal. But it happened, it's time to move on.

'I don't think I am looking to win anyone over or say anything that helps them get back on my side. It is what it is. There are reasons for it and I am just looking forward to playing for England and doing well and looking for success.'

White has spoken publicly about not watching football, and this, too, has been used against him by some supporters.

'It doesn't mean I don't like football, because I do it every day, so I must like it a little bit!' he said. 'It's because I don't watch it, which I think is quite normal for someone that does a job and then doesn't go home and then continue watching that job.

'I don't think anyone knows my personality. I keep that to my loved ones and family. It's all noise when people have an opinion on that stuff. It is not actually me. People don't know me and I like it that way. You give so much to the game, which is intense. I think it's important to keep some bits for yourself. I keep the energy for the people I love.'

England new boy Josh King believes his aptitude for academia gives him an advantage at the start of a burgeoning career.

The Fulham star – who has two Premier League goals this season – linked up with Thomas Tuchel’s squad on Monday morning as one of two fresh call ups alongside Jobe Bellingham.

King was a straight-A student at the renowned independent Hampton School in south London, with alumni including Queen guitarist Sir Brian May and golf pro Paul Casey.

The teenager’s mother, Michelle, is a university lecturer and finishing a PhD on sickle cell disease while his father Steve is a personal injury lawyer.

‘The most important thing about being good academically and education-wise is you’re able to take on information much more quickly,’ King said.

‘You can take on information which might take a bit longer for other people. I think that definitely helps.’

While the 19-year-old was invited to take part in a pre-World Cup training camp in Miami, he is hoping to make his international bow against Croatia on Thursday.

Although a central figure in Fulham’s midfield, a confident King could be utilised wider by Tuchel were he to be given an opportunity across the two-game Nations League camp.

‘There was a buzz around Fulham when I got called up [this week],’ he added. ‘I got off the phone with the boss, ran into the kitchen and started cheering with my dad. I called mum at work and she was screaming in the office. This felt slightly unachievable when I was younger.’

Jude Bellingham and Harry Kane were namechecked as those who helped him settle when introduced to the group in Florida, with King having also studied the likes of Jamal Musiala and Pedri to elevate his own game.

‘The main thing you take is the standards they set,’ he said when reflecting on joining the camp in America. ‘The way they conduct themselves, the way they perform every day, the way they train and the way they take everything seriously.

‘The little things, technical things, the way some people strike the ball, the way some people pass, just little things which you almost take without thinking about it - they also have an impact.

‘On a few occasions I asked “how do you finish this way” or “what do you do in this scenario” and it always helps to add to my game.’

Former Spurs striker Harry Kane has talked up Will Lankshear's season with Middlesbrough after joining from Tottenham Hotspur in the summer.

Simms has demonstrated his ability in the second tier, and could more than ease the pressure on Lankshear as a backup option, without requiring Hellberg to change his approach all too much.

From the player perspective too, a return to regular football could appeal to Simms if Boro can offer a role in their push for promotion.

As a result of Raul Jimenez's injury, Wolves are another club who may be looking to do business in the January transfer window, and Simms' name could crop up again after previous interest in the Coventry forward.

The Old Gold were linked with the striker over the summer alongside a series of Championship clubs, before no move materialised before the end of the window in September.

Wolves were also credited with interest in Lankshear in July, with Football London's Alasdair Gold reporting that a bid had been rejected, meaning that the Black Country club would surely be frustrated to see Boro secure another player linked with a move to Molineux - especially with the two clubs battling at the top end of the division.

For Middlesbrough, a move would make complete sense after their recent struggles, and the deal may just be right for Coventry, with it looking unlikely that the forward will see significant minutes in the battle for Premier League survival.

#2037. Stewart Downing reveals Adama Traore bad ‘habit’ at Middlesbrough

The former Middlesbrough star Stewart Downing has revealed one of the habits of his former teammate, Adama Traore, was always getting him in trouble.

Over a playing career which spanned more than 700 appearances over the course of two full decades, there wasn't much in the game that the former Middlesbrough winger Stewart Downing didn't get to witness. He won the League Cup twice, made over 400 Premier League appearances for four different clubs, and was capped 35 times by the England national team.

Since he called time on an illustrious playing career in August 2021, Downing moved into coaching and scouting. After a spell with the Boro youth academy, he moved to Leeds United in 2024 as an under-21 coach before returning to Liverpool as a Global Talent Scout in May 2025, but in a recent interview he was given a series of quick-fire questions about his playing career, and he recalled that one of this former teammates had a particular issue which hit the player concerned hard in the pocket.

Speaking on the No Tippy Tappy Football podcast, in a clip that they posted to the social media platform X, Downing was asked a series of quick-fire questions about his playing career, and in amongst a number of questions about his former teammates, managers and opponents, one of the players he formerly played with came in for a bit of a rinsing.

Downing was asked of the players that he'd played with before, "who was always getting fined?", and the former Boro star had an immediate reply: "Adama Traore. Daily. Great kid but he had a habit of being late."

Clubs often have systems of fines for players who misbehave or otherwise break their rules, and punctuality is considered very important. On one hand, this is done to instill greater discipline into them, but it's also a matter of impressing upon them the importance of respecting both their teammates who have turned up on time and acting as a reminder of how fortunate they are to be playing professional football in the first place.

The overall impact he made at his boyhood club after coming through the ranks cannot be understated.

Stewart Downing and Adama Traore were at opposite ends of their career trajectories when they spent time together at The Riverside Stadium.

Downing was born in Middlesbrough and graduated from their academy into the first team, making his debut for them in 2002. He left the club in 2009, and would play for Liverpool, Aston Villa and West Ham United before returning to the club in 2015.

Traore, on the other hand, was at the start of his senior professional career. Born in Spain, he'd come through the academy at Barcelona, but then transferred to Aston Villa before making his move to Teesside in 2016, following Boro's return to the Premier League.

Boro finished the 2016-17 season in 19th place in the top-flight and haven't returned since, but Traore impressed despite failing to score all season and was even linked with a possible move to Chelsea following his club's return to the Championship. Downing, meanwhile, made thirty appearances for Boro that season, scoring once and contributing three assists.

That move failed to materialise, but Traore did continue to enhance his reputation back in the EFL. He won Middlesbrough's Fans' Player of the Year, Young Player of the Year and Players' Player of the Year awards at the end of the 2017-18 season.

That summer he left The Riverside Stadium for Wolverhampton Wanderers, who'd just been promoted back to the Premier League, after they met an £18 million release clause in his contract. Downing, meanwhile, left the club for Blackburn Rovers in 2019.

This hasn't been the first time that Downing has spoken about Traore's issues with punctuality, either. In 2022, Teesside Live reported on how the England star had explained that these problems became so severe that they led to fines for being late being scrapped.

Downing explained at the time: "One time he went home for an afternoon nap and we were leaving on the coach for a game later on in the day. Garry Monk just told the driver to leave him, because he wasn’t waking up! He was a really good lad, and we all tried to help him as much as we could – we’d just be saying, 'Adama, come on… just set your alarm on your phone!'"

But none of this affected Traore's popularity at The Riverside Stadium, as was demonstrated by the awards that he won at the end of the 2017-18 season. His talent as a player clearly outweighed any issues over turning up on time. He wouldn't have lasted decade at the top of the professional game had that been the case.

#2036. Como vs Man Utd Preview: Team news, injuries, form and where to watch

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United travel to Italy on Wednesday, looking for their first win since September 10, when they face Como in the Champions League.

Michael Carrick’s side have struggled to build any momentum this season. Sunday’s 1-1 draw against Leeds extended their winless run to six matches in all competitions, leaving United with just seven points from their opening seven Premier League games.

The pressure on Carrick continues to grow. A win in Europe would offer some relief, but Como will not make life easy for Manchester United.

Manchester United began their Champions League campaign with a convincing 4-0 win over Sabah FK but lost 2-0 to Atletico Madrid in their next match. They have since drawn with Leeds, having also failed to beat Fulham and Tottenham in the Premier League.

Their last six matches across all competitions have produced three draws and three defeats. Michael Carrick is in pressure of his own and needs a response from his players, particularly with United sitting 13th in the Premier League and 13th in the Champions League standings.

Como have shown more promise. Cesc Fabregas’ side beat RB Leipzig 4-1 in Europe before losing 3-2 to Feyenoord. They returned to winning ways against Fiorentina, claiming a 3-1 victory that moved them up to fifth in Serie A, with 14 points from seven games.

Como have scored 11 goals and conceded eight across their last six matches in all competitions. United have struggled, with their only clean sheet of the season coming against Sabah FK.

The match takes place at the Stadio Giuseppe Sinigaglia on Wednesday, October 21, with kick-off at 5:45 pm BST.

#2034. Anis Mehmeti's Ipswich Town situation hasn't changed - Derby County, Birmingham City will watch

As the calendar year comes to an end, murmurs are starting to swirl around which fringe Premier League players may find themselves in the Championship during the January transfer window, and Ipswich Town's Anis Mehmeti has to be one of them.

The Albanian international was on the move last January, signing for the Tractor Boys midway through the campaign for a reported fee of just £3 million, with his contract at Bristol City in its final six months.

Mehmeti had established himself over the past 18 months at Ashton Gate as a top Championship attacking midfielder and someone on the fringes of making that jump to the Premier League, and he did so at Portman Road.

The 25-year-old's top-flight dreams have been quite nighmareish so far this season, though. He's yet to make a league squad for Gary O'Neil's side, who have enjoyed a decent start to life back in the Premier League without him.

As January approaches, he'll surely be someone touted for a short-term move to get some more game time, and a few of the sides who had interest in him this past summer should be retaining that.

Discussion surrounding whether Mehmeti would be able to make the step-up to the top flight with Ipswich was swirling back in the summer. With the 25-year-old not in Gary O'Neil's plans in the first month of the season, many were expecting him to leave by deadline day.

Derby County were interested, as were Hollywood duo Wrexham and Birmingham City, but the 25-year-old declined the option to move back to the Championship, instead choosing to fight for his place at Portman Road.

Mehmeti has appeared in just one game in all competitions for the Tractor Boys this season: their 4-2 defeat in the EFL Cup against Arsenal. He did score in that game, but the performance evidently wasn't enough to warrant even a place on the bench in a Premier League outing.

Two and a half months on from deciding to stay at Ipswich, that doesn't seem to have been the right choice. With the transfer window opening soon, he may find himself tempted to prove himself once more in the league below.

Both Derby and Birmingham have experienced a stark contrast in their starts to the Championship campaign.

Chris Davies' side sit atop the second tier at the November international break, while John Eustace was close to the sack before a relieving 3-1 win over Bolton Wanderers in the final game before the break - just their second victory this season, and their first at Pride Park.

The Rams are still inside the bottom three, but have some newfound belief that the campaign can be salvaged in the coming months, especially with the good news surrounding some starters returning from long-term absences, which have contributed to this poor start.

Despite being at opposite ends of the table, though, both sides could use someone like Mehmeti on their side for the second half of the campaign.

Where do you think Anis Mehmeti will be playing come the end of the January transfer window? Let us know in the comments.

We saw last season that the 25-year-old is comfortable coming into a side already challenging for promotion and settling in quite quickly. Mehmeti started 12 of the 19 games he was available for in Kieran McKenna's side last year, scoring and assisting twice.

Meanwhile, Derby need goals. The three against Bolton was just the third time this season they'd scored more than once in a game, and the first time since their last win at Portsmouth on September 1st. Mehmeti can provide that from an attacking midfield position.

John Eustace knows how to get the best out of a goalscoring attacking midfielder, as seen with Sammie Szmodics in the 2023/24 Championship campaign.

Even now at Derby, the Irishman has been the Rams' most dangerous goal threat this season, with four goals in 14 games before undergoing knee surgery earlier this month, which will see him out for around five more weeks.

With Mehmeti as a versatile option across the three behind a striker, bringing him in won't force Szmodics to watch on from the sidelines. They could easily both play together, and those two behind a returning Patrick Agyemeng could transform this Derby frontline in the new year.

The Albanian international netted 12 for Liam Manning's Bristol City side en route to the play-offs a couple of years ago, before starting last season with nine goals and six assists in all competitions at Ashton Gate ahead of his move to Ipswich.

If he can contribute with nearly as many goal contributions in the second half of this season, Derby won't need to worry about relegation at all.

Unfortunately, though, Derby's current concerning position in the Championship table may work against them in a potential Mehmeti deal, especially if a club like Birmingham look to reignite interest, too.

The 25-year-old wanted to prove he was Premier League quality back in August. If he's likely to do that elsewhere in January, a club near the top of the second tier would be an ideal place to showcase that.

If he can get promoted for a second time in as many years, there wouldn't be much more a player could do to be given a chance in the top flight, especially if he contributes to a strong second half of the season at that club.

At Derby, he'd be tasked with helping a club stave off relegation and climb up the table, which may not be as impressive on his resume after this season.

So, if the Rams are to put themselves in the best position possible to attract Mehemti or other leading Championship players who are on the fringes of the Premier League, a good run of form between now and the new year will help them out massively.

Michael Carrick will remain in charge of Manchester United for their Premier League trip to Chelsea on Saturday after the club decided to give their under-pressure head coach another opportunity to turn things around.

United have gone eight games without a win in all competitions, with Sunday’s humiliating 3-0 home defeat to Bournemouth leaving them 16th in the Premier League table, just one point above the relegation zone.

Carrick’s position looked increasingly uncertain after the Bournemouth loss. As a result, United chief executive Omar Berrada and director of football Jason Wilcox were at Carrington on Monday to discuss the situation.

#2032. Two days or two weeks – Michael Carrick future decision rolls into the international break

Manchester United will use the November international break to decide whether Michael Carrick stays on as head coach, after a three-game reprieve that delivered only one win.

According to The Guardian, Carrick came close to being sacked after the 3-0 home defeat to Bournemouth on 25 October.

Director of football Jason Wilcox and chief executive Omar Berrada discussed his position at the training ground the following day and agreed to give him three matches to turn things around before the break.

Those games brought a 2-0 defeat at Chelsea, a 3-2 win over Roma and a chaotic 4-4 draw with Aston Villa at Old Trafford on Sunday. United conceded eight goals across the three fixtures, and defensive frailty remains the biggest worry.

Wilcox is now leading a review of Carrick’s position. He will consult Berrada before making a recommendation to Sir Jim Ratcliffe, who oversees the club’s sporting operations. Talks are expected to begin on Monday and continue through the break.

The league table makes grim reading. United sit in the Premier League relegation zone with just one league win in their last 10 matches.

If he does win a stay of execution, results will need to follow. Manchester United cannot be languishing in 17th place.

Carrick remains defiant. Speaking to Sky Sports News after the Villa draw, he admitted his side “haven’t won enough games” but insisted he expects to move forward.

“I’m quite clear with what direction the club needs to go in, and it’s got to win games, said Carrick after the late Villa draw.

“I think there’s more than enough [to turn things around].”

If United do make a change, former Newcastle manager Eddie Howe is reported to be Wilcox’s preferred candidate. Howe has been out of work since leaving St James’ Park during pre-season, after four and a half years in charge.

In the meantime, there are no plans to strengthen Carrick’s coaching staff.

For now, Carrick is expected to be in charge for United’s next match, against Liverpool. Whether three games were enough to convince the hierarchy should become clear before then.

The trip to Anfield could be do or die for the decorated former United midfielder.

He’s not likely to survive a heavy defeat at the home of the club’s fierce rivals, but a galvanising win would surely buy him time and goodwill.

That’s if he makes it through the next fortnight before then. A decision or indication could be two days or two weeks away.

The only certainty at Old Trafford is uncertainty.
